No shares may be dematerialised or rematerialised 
between Wednesday, 2 December 2026 and Friday, 
4 December 2026, both days inclusive.
The South African dividend tax rate is 20% for 
shareholders who are liable to pay dividend tax, resulting 
in a final net cash dividend of ZA 52.00000 cents per 
share for these shareholders. Foreign investors may 
qualify for a lower dividend tax rate, subject to completion 
of a dividend taxation declaration and submission 
to Computershare Investor Services Proprietary 
Limited, MUFG Corporate Markets or Computershare 
Investor Services Proprietary Limited, who manage the 
South African, UK or Australian registers, respectively. 
The Company’s South African income taxation reference 
number is 9154588173. The proposed dividend will 
be paid out of the Company’s South African income 
reserves/retained earnings without drawing on any 
other capital reserves.
Dividend policy
Pan African aspires to pay a regular dividend to its 
shareholders and to balance this cash return for 
shareholders with the Group’s strategy of generic and 
acquisitive growth. We believe a target payout ratio 
of 40% to 50% of net cash generated from operating 
activities, after providing for the cash flow impact of 
capital expenditure (reduced by externally funded 
capital), contractual debt repayments and the cash flow 
impact of once-off items (discretionary ZAR cash flow), 
is appropriate. This measure aligns dividend distributions 
with the cash generation potential of the business. 
In proposing a dividend, the board will also take into 
account the Company’s financial position, prospects, 
satisfactory solvency and liquidity assessments and other 
factors deemed by the board to be relevant at the time. 
The net proposed dividend together with the approved 
share buy-back programme (as detailed as follows), 
constitutes a payout ratio of 31.8% of the Group’s 
discretionary cash flows, as defined by its dividend policy. 
The payout ratio is within the dividend policy guidelines, 
and the record dividend is indicative of the board’s 
assessment of the sustainability of the operations and the 
favourable prospects for FY27. The proposed dividend 
equates to a dividend yield of 3.6% in ZAR terms, 3.7% 
in GBP terms, based on the 30 June 2026 closing price 
of ZAR21.14 and GBP0.96 per share.
SHARE BUY-BACK PROGRAMME
Pan African is pleased to announce that the board has 
approved a share buy-back programme to purchase 
up to ZAR500 million (approximately US$30.4 million) 
of ordinary shares of GBP0.01 each in the Company, 
commencing during October 2026. The Company’s 
profits available for distribution exceed the maximum 
amount proposed to be paid by the Company in 
implementing the buy-back programme. 
The board believes that, at the current share price, 
the Company’s shares offer significant value, given the 
quality and profitability of the Group’s existing operations 
and growth projects. The board has therefore taken 
the decision to implement the programme as part 
of the Company’s broader strategy to deliver value 
to shareholders. 
Purchases pursuant to the programme will be made:
• under the authority granted by shareholders at the 
Company’s 2025 AGM (repurchase authority). The 
repurchase authority permits the purchase of the 
Company’s shares at a maximum price (excluding 
expenses) of 105% of (i) the average closing price of 
such shares traded on the LSE or (ii) the weighted 
average market price of such shares traded on the 
JSE, for the five business days immediately preceding 
the date of purchase
• in accordance with the UK version of the 
Market Abuse Regulation 596/2014 and the 
Commission Delegated Regulation (EU) 2016/1052 
(each as in force in the UK by virtue of the European 
Union (Withdrawal) Act 2018 and as amended by 
the Market Abuse Regulation (Amendment) (EU Exit) 
Regulations 2019) and the JSE Listings Requirements 
(to the extent required)
• on the Main Market of the LSE and the JSE. Shares 
acquired on the JSE will be in accordance with the 
Market Abuse Regulation to maintain consistency 
between exchanges
• in compliance with the relevant conditions for trading, 
restrictions regarding time and volume, disclosure 
and reporting obligations and price conditions. The 
shares will be acquired at a price (excluding expenses) 
that does not exceed the last independent trade or 
the highest current independent bid on the relevant 
trading platform. 
The Company intends to cancel those shares acquired 
pursuant to the programme. 

--- S020 | original physical page 14 | text selection | Commentary ---
Pan African will enter into an agreement with 
Peel Hunt LLP to carry out purchases pursuant to 
the programme. Purchases of shares held on the 
Company’s:
• UK register will be implemented on-market through the 
LSE, where Peel Hunt LLP will act as principal, and 
• South African register will be implemented on-market 
through the LSE, by way of a two-limb structure, 
where Peel Hunt LLP will act as principal. In this 
regard, Peel Hunt LLP, acting as principal, will acquire 
the shares through the JSE order book and sell such 
shares to the Company, on market through the LSE. 
Purchases will not be initiated on the ASX, however, 
holders of CD’s will be able to participate in the buy-back 
by transferring their CDI holdings into shares on either the 
UK or South African registers.
The agreement will grant Peel Hunt LLP the authority to 
enact purchases and make trading decisions concerning 
the timing of the purchases under the programme 
independently and uninfluenced by the Company during 
any closed period to which the Company is subject 
and/or if the Company comes into possession of inside 
information (prohibited period), subject to the Company 
having submitted a repurchase programme to the JSE 
ahead of entering into a prohibited period in accordance 
with the JSE Listings Requirements. In accordance 
with the JSE Listings Requirements, the Company 
has submitted a repurchase programme to the JSE to 
enable purchases pursuant to the programme during a 
prohibited period. 
Details of any purchases made under the programme 
will be provided via the Regulatory News Service in the 
UK (RNS), the Stock Exchange News Service of the 
JSE (SENS) and ASX announcements and published 
on the Company’s website.

OUTLOOK AND PROSPECTS
Our primary focus for the short term is safely delivering 
into our production guidance and successfully executing 
capital projects that will sustain and increase future gold 
production. 
In particular, we will: 
• continue our focus on health and safety initiatives 
in our proactive journey to ‘zero harm’
• focus on achieving production and cost guidance 
• execute capital projects designed to sustain and 
increase future gold production
• continue the Group’s ESG initiatives and advance 
our renewable energy roadmap as part of the 
decarbonisation and cost reduction strategy
• maintain focus on generating sustainable shareholder 
returns with increased dividends balanced with 
prudent capital allocation for expansion
• explore local and international growth opportunities 
in a responsible and circumspect manner.
FORWARD-LOOKING INFORMATION
Any forward-looking information contained in this 
announcement is the sole responsibility of the directors 
and has not been reviewed or reported on by the Group’s 
external auditors.

as stipulated under the Market Abuse Regulation (EU) 
596/2014 as it forms part of UK Domestic Law by virtue 
of the European Union (Withdrawal) Act 2018. Upon the 
publication of this announcement via the RNS, SENS and 
the ASX, this inside information is now considered to be 
in the public domain.
Appreciation
I would like to thank our motivated leadership, dedicated 
staff and contractors for their unwavering commitment 
to the ongoing success and sustainability of the Group.
I am grateful for the support and guidance from our 
trusted board in navigating challenges and opportunities 
in preparing for the exciting expansion of our horizons 
in the future.
The information contained in this announcement is the 
responsibility of the Company’s board and has not been 
reviewed or reported on by the Group’s external auditors.

### [SECTION READING RESPONSE / DETERMINATION]
### 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

- **Q5: Verify Tennant FY27 guidance 48,000–52,000oz vs FY26 32,124oz; confirm capex for fixed crusher, secondary mill, belt filter; White Devil first blast Aug 2026 and grade ramp. [D002, p.3]**
  - **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D006/D009)`
  - **Answer & evidence**: FY27 guidance of 48,000–52,000oz is confirmed verbatim in D003 [D003, physical PDF p. 6]. FY26 Tennant production of 32,124oz is confirmed in the production table [D003, physical PDF p. 6]. Capex items are named — "fixed crusher circuit, secondary mill and a belt filter for dry-stack tailings" — but **no dollar amounts are given** in this section [D003, physical PDF p. 6]. The "White Devil first blast Aug 2026" date and grade ramp are **not stated** in the captured D003 text; only that FY27 will have "almost a full year of mining from the high-grade White Devil deposit" [D003, physical PDF p. 5]. Remainder (capex quantum, first-blast date, grade ramp) stays open for D006/D009.

- **Q7: Verify Evander underground grade >11g/t FY26 (vs 6.8g/t FY25) sustainability into FY27; is it repeatable or one-off? [D002, p.3]**
  - **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D009)`
  - **Answer & evidence**: D003 confirms Evander FY26 production of 46,855oz vs FY25 27,829oz [D003, physical PDF p. 6], consistent with a grade step-up, but the captured D003 text does **not** state the >11g/t grade figure or discuss its sustainability. The Evander narrative appears to fall on pages not fully captured in this selection. Grade sustainability remains open for D009.

- **Q10: Confirm Royal Sheba first production timing (first blast early 2027) and whether any FY27 ounces; confirm not double-counted with BTRP. [D002, p.3]**
  - **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D009)`
  - **Answer & evidence**: D003 confirms the BTRP crusher circuit is being designed "to allow for the processing of hard rock from Royal Sheba" and that BTRP's remaining LoM is "six years from the current surface sources" [D003, physical PDF p. 6]. However, **no first-blast date and no FY27 Royal Sheba ounces are stated** in the captured text. The double-counting question is not addressed. Remains open for D009.

*(Stored questions Q13, Q3, Q4, Q8, Q12, Q2, Q11, Q1, Q6, Q9 relate to sections not in this upload — carried forward in Section 4.)*

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

No footnote markers (`[^1]`, `*`, note references) or small-print footnote definitions appear in the captured D003 evidence. The qualifications that matter here are **embedded bullet commentary**, which function as de-facto footnotes:

- **MTR calcine layer** [D003, physical PDF p. 6]: "mining through the last of the calcine material will be completed during the FY27Q1 production period, after which grade and recoveries will normalise." This is a **hidden near-term constraint**: FY27 H1 (at least Q1) MTR grade/recovery remains depressed, so the 50,000→60,000oz/yr capacity uplift from the Dec-2025 plant expansion will **not** be fully realised until Q2 FY27. Forecast implication: model MTR FY27 below the ~60Koz nameplate, with a step-up from Q2. Must verify in D009 whether FY27 MTR guidance reflects this phasing.
- **BTRP recovery/grade decline** [D003, physical PDF p. 6]: recovery 33.0% (FY25 42.1%), recovered grade 0.5g/t (FY25 0.7g/t) from 775,385t (FY25 725,535t). This is a **structural degradation of feed**, not a one-off — the flotation circuit (US$5.9m, complete end-CY2026) is the mitigation. Forecast implication: BTRP FY27 ounces likely remain depressed in H1 FY27 until flotation commissioning; verify in D009 whether BTRP FY27 guidance assumes flotation benefit.
- **Elikhulu Winkelhaak feed from FY27** [D003, physical PDF p. 6]: new feed source adds flexibility but no volume guidance given. Verify in D009 whether Elikhulu FY27 guidance exceeds FY26's 56,475oz.
- **Tennant grade blend** [D003, physical PDF p. 6]: grade rose from 1.3g/t (CPS only) to 1.5g/t (blend with Weaber's Find, Rising Sun, Nobles). This underpins the FY27 48–52Koz guidance but the **White Devil grade contribution is not quantified** — verify in D009.
- **"Almost a full year of mining from White Devil"** [D003, physical PDF p. 5]: implies a partial-year contribution in FY27 (i.e., not full 12 months), consistent with a mid-FY27 start. Verify exact start month in D009.

### [SECTION READING RESPONSE / DETERMINATION]
### 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

**Q13: Confirm Emmerson acquisition consolidation date (22 June 2026), purchase consideration, goodwill/PPA, FY27 contribution. [D002, p.3]**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D006)`
- **Answer & evidence**: The statement of financial position shows a new **"Shares to be issued"** line of **US$148,218k** in FY26 (nil FY25) `[D005.1, physical PDF p. 17]`, and the statement of changes in equity confirms this was recognised as a non-cash equity-settled addition in FY26 `[D005.1, physical PDF p. 19]`. This is consistent with an acquisition settled in shares (Emmerson) rather than cash — note the cash flow statement shows **no acquisition cash outflow** other than "Cash acquired on acquisitions" of US$12,796k `[D005.1, physical PDF p. 18]`. However, the **consolidation date (22 June 2026), the split of consideration between shares/cash, the goodwill/PPA allocation, and the FY27 contribution are NOT disclosed in this section** — they sit in the business-combinations note (note 18). Goodwill rose only US$1,340k (17,098→18,438) `[D005.1, p. 17]`, which is small relative to a US$148m share consideration, so the PPA/bargain-purchase treatment must be verified in D006. **Remainder stays open for D006.**

*(No other stored questions are answerable from this balance-sheet/P&L/cash-flow/equity capture — all others carried forward to Section 4.)*

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

- **Cash flow footnote [^1] — dividend re-presentation** `[D005.1, physical PDF p. 18]`: *"The Group changed the presentation of reciprocal dividend cash flows from gross to net during the current reporting period. The comparative figures have been re-presented accordingly."*
  - **What it changes**: The "Dividend paid" line of **(58,540)** FY26 vs (23,675) FY25 is a **net** figure, not gross dividends to all shareholders. It nets off the reciprocal dividend received on treasury shares held by PAR Gold (13.1% holder). This means the cash-flow dividend line **cannot** be reconciled directly to the equity "Dividends paid" of (67,387) `[D005.1, p. 19]` without adding back the reciprocal dividend of 8,847. Check: 67,387 − 8,847 = 58,540 ✓ — arithmetic ties. **Implication for forecast**: model dividend cash outflow on a net basis; do not gross up. Verify in D006 (note 22 related party) that the reciprocal dividend is a genuine cash round-trip and not a permanent exclusion.

- **Equity footnote [^1] — Reciprocal dividend – PAR Gold** `[D005.1, physical PDF p. 19]`: PAR Gold holds **13.1%** of issued share capital; the reciprocal dividend (8,847 FY26; 3,784 FY25) is an intra-group dividend on treasury shares. **Implication**: this is a related-party circular flow that inflates both dividend paid and a retained-earnings credit; it should be eliminated in a clean free-cash-flow-to-equity view. Verify note 22.

- **Equity footnotes [^2]/[^3]/[^4] — numbering mismatch** `[D005.1, physical PDF p. 19]`: The table uses markers [^1], [^2], [^3], [^4] but the printed footnote definitions are labelled [^1], [^3], [^4] with **no [^2] definition visible** and the "Refer to note 15" / "Refer to note 14" mapping is ambiguous (share buy-back and shares-to-be-issued both tagged [^3]). **This is a capture/presentation gap** — the exact note cross-references for capital reduction, buy-back and shares-to-be-issued must be confirmed in D006 (notes 14/15). Do not assume.

- **"Shares to be issued" US$148,218k** `[D005.1, p. 17 & 19]`: A large non-cash equity item with **no cash flow**. It sits in equity but is not yet share capital — dilution/issuance mechanics and whether these are Emmerson consideration shares must be confirmed (note 14/15, D006). This materially affects **share count for FY27 EPS and dividend per share**.

- **Share-based payment obligations jump**: non-current 10,297→20,385 and current 11,190→43,969 (total ~64.4m vs ~21.5m) `[D005.1, p. 17]`. This is a large balance-sheet build; the P&L charge sits in "Other expenses" (85,958 vs 36,484) — quantify in D006 (note 17) for Q12.

- **Deferred tax liabilities** 140,506→250,480 (+~110m) `[D005.1, p. 17]` alongside a deferred tax **asset** of only 1,921 — a large timing difference build (likely PPE/EMA); relevant to FY27 effective tax rate modelling. Verify in note 6.2.

1. BASIS OF PREPARATION
The accounting policies applied in compiling the 
summarised consolidated financial statements, 
in accordance with IFRS Accounting Standards as 
issued by the IASB, are consistent with those applied in 
preparing the Group’s financial statements for the year 
ended 30 June 2025. There are no material differences 
between UK-adopted International Accounting Standards 
and IFRS Accounting Standards as applied to these 
financial statements.
The financial information set out in this announcement 
does not constitute the Company’s statutory accounts 
for the period ended 30 June 2026. 
Furthermore, these financial statements have 
been prepared in accordance with the SAICA 
Financial Reporting Guidelines as issued by the 
Accounting Practices Committee, Financial Reporting 
Pronouncements as issued by the Financial Reporting 
Standards Council, the UK Listing Rules, the JSE Listings 
Requirements and the UK Companies Act 2006.
Going concern
The Group closely monitors and manages its liquidity 
risk by means of a centralised treasury function. Cash 
forecasts are regularly produced and sensitivities run for 
different scenarios including, but not limited to, changes 
in commodity prices and different production profiles 
from the Group’s producing assets. The Group had 
US$78.9 million (FY25: US$50.2 million) of available debt 
facilities and US$246.2 million (FY25: US$49.5 million) of 
cash and cash equivalents and short-term investments 
as at 30 June 2026. The Group has considered the 
going concern forecast through to 30 June 2028, using 
a semi-static gold price in the base case scenario. 
The base case scenario assumes an initial gold price of 
US$4,600/oz increasing to US$4,900/oz for the next 
reporting period. For the downside scenario, a gold 
price of US$3,680/oz was applied, together with a 10% 
reduction in production. The Group’s forecasts, based 
on the board-approved budgets (with production in line 
with production guidance announced), demonstrate it will 
have sufficient liquidity headroom to meet its obligations, 
under both scenarios.
The board has a reasonable expectation that the Group 
has adequate resources to continue in operational 
existence for the foreseeable future. Accordingly, the 
Group continues to adopt the going concern basis 
of accounting in preparation of the 30 June 2026 
financial statements.
Alternative performance measures
The Group makes reference to APMs, in conjunction with 
IFRS Accounting Standards measures, when assessing 
its reported financial performance, financial position and 
cash flows. APMs should be considered in addition to, 
and not as a substitute for or superior to, measures of 
financial performance, financial position or cash flows 
reported in accordance with IFRS Accounting Standards. 
Further information on APMs is provided on page 72.
2. JUDGEMENTS AND ESTIMATES
The preparation of the financial statements in accordance 
with UK-adopted International Accounting Standards 
and IFRS Accounting Standards requires management to 
make judgements, estimates and assumptions that may 
materially affect the application of the Group’s accounting 
policies and the reported amounts of assets, liabilities, 
income and expenses.
These judgements and estimates are based on 
management’s best knowledge of the relevant facts 
and circumstances, historical experience, current and 
expected future economic conditions and other factors. 
Actual results may differ from the amounts included in 
the financial statements.
The estimates and underlying assumptions are reviewed 
on an ongoing basis. Revisions to accounting estimates 
are recognised prospectively.
Significant assumptions and estimates
The following areas contain information about significant 
assumptions and other sources of estimation uncertainty 
as at 30 June 2026 that have a significant risk of resulting 
in a material adjustment to the carrying amounts of 
assets and liabilities in the next reporting period.
Deferred tax rates applied within the Group
South African income tax on gold mining income is 
determined according to the gold formula that takes 
into account the taxable income and revenue from 
gold mining operations. Judgement was applied in 
determining the future expected deferred tax rates of 
the Group’s mining operations.
Deferred tax is calculated at the tax rates that are 
expected to apply to the period when the asset is 
realised, or the liability is settled, based on tax rates and 
laws that have been enacted or substantively enacted by 
the reporting date. The rates used to calculate deferred 
tax are based on the current estimate of future profitability 

--- S002 | original physical page 20 | text selection | Mixed / unspecified ---
when temporary differences will be utilised. The 
respective rates are calculated based on management’s 
best estimate through which the temporary difference will 
be realised over the life of the mining operations.
Other assumptions and estimates
Cash flow projections and key assumptions
As part of the assessment of potential impairment 
indicators, the group prepares indicative valuation models 
for certain cash generating units. Expected future cash 
flows used in discounted cash flow models are inherently 
uncertain and could materially change over time. Cash 
flow projections are significantly affected by a number of 
factors including Mineral Resources and Mineral Reserves 
together with economic factors such as commodity prices, 
foreign exchange rates and discount rates and estimates 
of production costs and future capital expenditure.
Cash flow projections are based on financial forecasts 
and LoM plans incorporating key assumptions as 
detailed below:
• Mineral Resources and Mineral Reserves:
Mineral Reserves and, where considered appropriate, 
Mineral Resources reflected within projected cash flows 
are based on Mineral Resources and Mineral Reserves 
statements (in accordance with the SAMREC Code for 
South African properties) and exploration and evaluation 
work undertaken by appropriately qualified persons. 
Mineral Resources are included where management 
has a high degree of confidence in their economic 
extraction, despite additional evaluation still being 
required prior to meeting the required confidence to 
convert to Mineral Reserves
• Commodity prices: Commodity prices are based 
on the latest internal forecasts, benchmarked with 
external sources of information, to ensure that they 
are within the range of available analyst forecasts. 
Where existing sales contracts are in place, the effects 
of such contracts or hedging arrangements are 
considered in determining future cash flows
• Discount rates: Value in use and fair value less cost 
of disposal projections are sensitive to changes in the 
discount rate
• Operating costs, capital expenditure and other 
operating factors: Operating costs and capital 
expenditure are based on financial budgets. Cash 
flow projections are based on LoM plans and internal 
management forecasts. Cost assumptions incorporate 
management experience and expectations, as well 
as the nature and location of the operation and the 
risk associated therewith (for example, the grade 
of Mineral Resources and Mineral Reserves varying 
significantly over time and unforeseen operational 
issues).
Rehabilitation obligation
The amount recognised as an obligation represents 
management’s best estimate of the consideration 
required to complete the restoration and rehabilitation 
activity. These estimates are inherently uncertain and 
could materially change over time.
At each reporting date, the Group estimates the 
environmental rehabilitation obligation. There is 
judgement in the assumptions used in determining the 
estimated obligation which include:
• closure costs, which are determined in accordance 
with regulatory requirements
• the inflation rate which has been adjusted for a
long-term view
• the risk-free rate, which is compounded annually 
and linked to the LoM
• the LoM and related Mineral Resources and 
Mineral Reserves.
An assessment of the Group’s environmental rehabilitation 
plan identified a risk relating to the potential pollution 
of groundwater at Barberton Mines. As a result of the 
amendments to the Financial Closure Provision Regulations 
promulgated in terms of the National Environmental 
Management Act, 107 of 1998, the Group is required 
to include an obligation for all latent and residual 
environmental liabilities, including water pollution, as 
part of the obligation for environmental rehabilitation and 
decommissioning costs. The Group has undertaken several 
detailed assessments, including a geohydrological study 
at Barberton Mines, to ascertain the latent and residual 
environmental liability as a result of the amendments and 
to quantify the impact of the amendments. Based on the 
current closure cost estimate, the amendments will result 
in an increase to the current obligation of approximately 
US$3.0 million (US$0.7 million on a discounted basis) for 
environmental and decommissioning costs in real terms, 
once the amendments become effective. The effective 
date of the amendments is yet to be determined. Given 
the uncertainty, no obligation has been recognised at the 
reporting date.
While the Group is not a member of the International 
Council on Mining and Metals, it continues to assess 
and progressively align the management of its TSFs 
with the requirements of the GISTM. This work includes 
assessments of the Group’s TSFs against the applicable 
GISTM requirements and the identification of actions, 
where required, to address identified gaps.
These assessments remain ongoing and the nature, 
scope and timing of any additional measures that may be 
required have not yet been fully determined. Accordingly, 
at the reporting date, the Group is not able to reliably 
estimate any incremental future expenditure that may 
arise specifically from further alignment with the GISTM. 
Furthermore, the assessments completed to date have 
not identified additional present obligations requiring 
recognition beyond those already incorporated, where 
applicable, in the Group’s existing rehabilitation and closure 
provisions. No additional provision has therefore been 
recognised specifically in respect of GISTM alignment.
The Group will continue to assess the implications of the 
GISTM as the programme progresses and will recognise 
or disclose any additional obligations in accordance with 
the applicable accounting requirements when sufficient 
information becomes available. Further information 
regarding the Group’s tailings management practices and 
progress towards GISTM conformance is available on 
our website at https//www.panafricanresources.com/
pan-african-resources-tailings-management-system/

It states South African gold mining income tax is determined by the "gold formula" taking into account taxable income and revenue from gold mining operations, and that judgement was applied in determining future expected deferred tax rates of the Group's mining operations. ### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

No footnote markers (`[^1]`, `[^2]`, `*`) or footnote definitions appear in the supplied D006.1 text `[D006.1, physical PDF p. 19–20]`. The material qualifications are instead embedded in the going-concern and judgements narrative:

- **Going concern — gold price deck (critical forecast input)** `[D006.1, physical PDF p. 19]`: Base case assumes **initial gold price US$4,600/oz rising to US$4,900/oz** for the next reporting period; downside case **US$3,680/oz with a 10% production reduction**. Forecast horizon extends to **30 June 2028**. This is a semi-static deck, not spot. *Implication*: management's own liquidity test is run well above the ~US$1,867/oz group AISC and above the DFS/study price assumptions flagged in Q8 — so the going-concern conclusion is not a stress test at current spot if spot is below US$4,600. Must verify the actual FY26 realised gold price and the FY27 budget price deck in D008/D009 to see whether the forecast model should use US$4,600 or a lower/market deck.
- **Liquidity headroom** `[D006.1, physical PDF p. 19]`: Available debt facilities **US$78.9m** (FY25: US$50.2m) and cash + short-term investments **US$246.2m** (FY25: US$49.5m). The large YoY jump in liquidity (cash+STI up ~US$197m) needs reconciling to the cash flow statement and to the Emmerson acquisition/equity raise — verify in D005/D006 cash note.
- **Deferred tax judgement** `[D006.1, physical PDF p. 19–20]`: Gold-formula tax + judgement on realisation timing = DTL is estimate-sensitive. This is the mechanism behind the +110m DTL move (Q26); the quantum must come from the deferred tax note.
- **Impairment / valuation sensitivity** `[D006.1, physical PDF p. 20]`: Cash flow projections for CGU impairment testing are "significantly affected" by Mineral Resources/Reserves, commodity prices, FX, discount rates, production costs and future capex. *Implication*: any FY26 impairment or reversal is a non-cash one-off that must be excluded from underlying forecast; verify whether any impairment was booked and its drivers in the PPE/impairment note.

### [FORENSIC EXTRACTION: CAPTURED TEXT & FINANCIAL TABLES]
--- Section D006.2 | original physical page 20 | text selection | Commentary ---
3. SEGMENT ANALYSIS
Operating segments are reported in a manner consistent 
with the internal reporting provided to the chief operating 
decision-maker. The chief operating decision-maker, 
which is responsible for allocating resources and 
assessing performance of the operating segments, has 
been identified as the Pan African executive committee 
(Exco). The operating segments of the Group are 
determined based on the reports used to make strategic 
decisions that are reviewed by Exco. Exco considers 
the business principally according to the location and 
nature of the products and services provided, with each 
segment representing a strategic business unit.
The reported segments comprise the following:
Mining operations
These segments derive their revenue from mining, 
extraction, production and the sale of gold.
South African operations
• Barberton Mines including the BTRP located in 
Barberton
• Evander Mines: Elikhulu, the underground 8 Shaft 
pillar, 24, 25 and 26 Level project, Egoli project and 
surface sources) located in Evander
• MTR operation: The MTR operation is located in 
the Mogale district. The plant was commissioned in 
October 2024 to process gold tailings deposits of 
Mogale Gold Proprietary Limited (Mogale Gold) and 
Mintails SA Soweto Cluster Proprietary Limited (MSC)
• Solar projects consist of the solar plant located at 
Evander Mines, the solar plant at Barberton Mines and 
the extension of Evander Mines’ solar plant. The solar 
plants were sold at arm’s length to the respective mines 
on 30 June 2026. Following completion of the disposal, 
the Group no longer has any operating activities or 
assets attributable to this segment and the segment 
will no longer be separately reported from 1 July 2026.
Australian operations
• Tennant Mines is located in the Northern Territory 
and Yungatha Asset Holdings Proprietary Limited 
(Yungatha) complements the Group’s current portfolio 
of high-margin, long-life surface remining operations. 
The segment includes Yungatha, which operates 
a motel in the Tennant Creek region to support the 
workforce requirements of local mining companies 
and other contractors, including Tennant Consolidated 
Mining Group Proprietary Limited (Tennant company) 
employees. In addition, the segment also includes 
Emmerson as of 22 June 2026; refer to note 18.1. 
Emmerson is a mineral exploration and royalty 
company engaged in the exploration and evaluation 
of mineral properties. The segment results include 
the assets and liabilities of Emmerson. No income or 
expenses have been included in the segment results 
for the reporting period, as there was no material 
income or expenditure arising from Emmerson 
between the acquisition date and the reporting date.
Other operations
• Exploration assets consist of five prospecting 
concessions (or exploration licences) in north-eastern 
Sudan (the Block 12 concessions), covering an area 
of almost 1,100km² and located approximately 70km 
north-west of Port Sudan
• Agricultural ESG projects mainly comprise the 
Group’s Barberton Blueberries project (Barberton 
Blue Proprietary Limited), as well as other small￾scale agricultural projects in Barberton Mines’ host 
community areas
• Corporate consists mainly of the Group’s holding 
companies and management services company 
which renders services to the Group and is located in 
Johannesburg
• Pan African Resources Funding Company Limited 
(Funding Company) is the centralised treasury 
function of the Group located in Johannesburg.

### [SECTION READING RESPONSE / DETERMINATION]
### 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

**Q26 (remainder) — Quantify deferred tax liability +110m drivers; extract FY27 expected effective tax rate.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D006.1 / D006 tax note)`
- **Answer & evidence**: This section provides the segment-level income tax expense but not the deferred tax note. FY26 group income tax expense = US$177,457k `[D006.2, physical PDF p. 21]`, split: Barberton (50,997), Evander (77,439), Solar (1,996), MTR (38,605), Tennant (8,512), Mining ops total (177,549), Corporate +809, Funding (717). Implied group effective tax rate on PBT 534,368 = **33.2%** (177,457/534,368). FY25 comparative: tax (56,028) on PBT 196,633 = **28.5%**. The +110m DTL driver and FY27 expected ETR are **not** in this section — carry forward to the deferred tax note.

**Q27 (remainder) — Resolve note 14/15 cross-reference and undefined [^2] footnote numbering gap.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: In this section footnotes are numbered per-page and are all defined: p.21 `[^1]` (other expenses/income exclude intra-Group management fees; finance income/costs exclude intra-Group interest) `[D006.2, p. 21]`; p.22 `[^1]`–`[^3]` all defined `[D006.2, p. 22]`; p.23 `[^1]`–`[^2]` defined `[D006.2, p. 23]`; p.24 `[^1]`–`[^4]` defined `[D006.2, p. 24]`. No numbering gap in D006.2. The earlier [^2] gap in D006.1 remains open.

**Q28 (remainder) — Confirm split of cash vs short-term investments 137,867 vs investments 12,791 vs restricted cash 2,612; reconcile to US$246.2m cash+STI.**
- **Status**: `Not resolved here (no balance-sheet detail in D006.2)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Segment assets/liabilities are disclosed but not the cash/STI split. Carry forward.

**Q29 — Confirm drivers of contract liability 8,030 and other receivables 41,654.**
- **Status**: `Not resolved here`
- **Answer & evidence**: Not disclosed in D006.2. Carry forward.

**Q30 — Confirm FY27 budget gold price deck and FX assumption.**
- **Status**: `Not resolved here`
- **Answer & evidence**: No price/FX deck in D006.2. Carry forward.

**Q31 — Reconcile cash+STI US$246.2m and debt facilities US$78.9m to cash flow statement and Emmerson/equity issuance.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (indirect)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Funding Company segment shows FY26 finance income 4,891 and finance costs (11,184), net (6,524) pre-tax `[D006.2, p. 21]`; FY25 Funding Company finance costs (18,125) vs income 1,574 `[D006.2, p. 23]`. This is consistent with materially lower group net finance costs in FY26 (group finance costs 17,318 vs 21,073 FY25; finance income 5,848 vs 1,856 FY25) `[D006.2, p. 21, 23]`. Direct reconciliation to cash flow statement still open.

**Q32 — Extract deferred tax note — quantify +110m DTL drivers and FY27 effective tax rate.**
- **Status**: `Not resolved here`
- **Answer & evidence**: Only current tax expense by segment visible. Carry forward.

**Q33 — Check PPE/impairment note for FY26 impairment/reversal, CGU carrying values, discount rate/price assumptions.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: FY26 impairment loss on non-financial assets = US$345k, all in Agricultural ESG projects `[D006.2, p. 21–22]`. FY25 comparative = US$2,954k (Evander 27, Exploration 2,927) `[D006.2, p. 23–24]`. No CGU-level impairment/reversal or discount-rate/price assumptions in this section — carry forward to PPE note.

**Q34 — Confirm page 72 APM reconciliation captured in D008; AISC/APM definitions tie to IFRS.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved`

Definitions differ between years: FY26 footnote [^3] adjusts only for impairment losses on non-financial assets `[D006.2, p. 22]`, whereas FY25 footnote [^4] adjusts for impairment, bargain purchase gains and unrealised fair value losses on derivatives `[D006.2, p. 24]`. This definitional inconsistency must be checked against D008 APM definitions.

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

- **p.21 `[^1]`** — "Other expenses and income exclude intra-Group management fees. Finance income and finance costs exclude intra-Group interest." `[D006.2, p. 21]`. This means the segment PBT lines are **before** intra-group management fee allocations and intra-group interest; the second block of the table (management fees, finance income/(costs), dividend eliminations) reconciles segment PBT to group PBT of 356,911. Implication: segment margins computed from the first block overstate true segment profitability for segments that are net payers of management fees (Barberton −5,687, Evander −2,831, Tennant −3,188) and understate for Corporate (+12,860 received). **Verification needed**: confirm in D008 whether APM segment costs (e.g., AISC by operation) are stated before or after these intra-group allocations.

- **p.22 `[^1]`** — "The segment assets and liabilities above exclude intra-Group balances." `[D006.2, p. 22]`. Segment net assets (excluding goodwill) sum to 1,017,559; group goodwill 18,438 sits entirely in Barberton. Implication: segment ROIC/asset-turn calculations must add back intra-group funding (Funding Company net assets 137,658; Corporate 43,459) to reconcile to group. **Verification**: check D005.1 balance sheet total equity vs 1,017,559 + 18,438 = 1,035,997.

- **p.22 `[^2]`** — "Capital expenditure comprises additions to property, plant and equipment, mineral rights and exploration assets." `[D006.2, p. 22]`. FY26 capex = US$223,995k vs FY25 US$167,909k (+33%). Segment split FY26: Barberton 40,081; Evander 70,941; Solar 7,159; MTR 32,599; Tennant 71,304; Corporate 1,833. Implication: **Tennant capex of US$71,304k in FY26 is 32% of group capex** — this is the Tennant expansion (crusher/mill/filter per Q16) and must be checked against FY27 capex guidance. Solar capex 7,159 despite disposal on 30 June 2026 — confirm no FY27 solar capex.

- **p.22 `[^3]` vs p.24 `[^4]` — Adjusted EBITDA definition inconsistency.** FY26 definition adjusts only for impairment losses on non-financial assets `[D006.2, p. 22]`; FY25 definition also adjusts for bargain purchase gains and unrealised fair value losses on derivatives `[D006.2, p. 24]`. Implication: FY26 vs FY25 Adjusted EBITDA (609,431 vs 226,584) is **not like-for-like** — FY25 includes a −28,019 bargain purchase gain reversal and +1,925 derivative loss reversal that FY26 does not. **Verification**: confirm in D008 which definition is used for covenant/APM purposes and restate FY25 on FY26 basis (FY25 EBITDA 249,724 + impairment 2,954 = 252,678 on FY26 definition, vs reported Adjusted EBITDA 226,584).

- **p.23 `[^1]`** — "Tennant Mines includes Tennant company and Yungatha. Tennant company was acquired in November 2024 and the results are for an eight-month period. Yungatha was acquired in December 2024 and the results are for a seven-month period." `[D006.2, p. 23]`. Implication: **FY25 Tennant revenue of only US$3,873k and PBT US$30,396k (including 28,019 bargain gain) is a partial-period, non-annualised figure**. FY26 Tennant revenue 132,804 and PBT 23,836 are the first full-year comparatives. Any FY27 Tennant forecast must be built off FY26, not FY25. **Verification**: confirm in D009 the Tennant FY26 production/grade and FY27 guidance to anchor the run-rate.

- **p.24 Geographical information** — Non-current assets: South Africa 911,962 (FY25 740,819); Australia 365,207 (FY25 155,716); Other 480. `[D006.2, p. 24]`. Implication: **Australia non-current assets more than doubled (+US$209m)** — this is Tennant expansion capex plus Emmerson assets. Confirms the Australian asset base is now material (28.6% of group non-current assets) and must be depreciated in FY27 forecasts. **Verification**: check PPE note for Australian depreciation run-rate and Emmerson asset fair value.

- **Solar projects disposal** — "The solar plants were sold at arm's length to the respective mines on 30 June 2026. Following completion of the disposal, the Group no longer has any operating activities or assets attributable to this segment and the segment will no longer be separately reported from 1 July 2026." `[D006.2, p. 20]`. Implication: FY26 Solar segment (revenue nil, loss −2,448 pre-tax, capex 7,159, net assets −6,962) **disappears from FY27**. The intra-group sale means the mines now bear the solar electricity cost directly — check whether FY27 electricity cost per segment steps up. **Verification**: confirm in D008/D009 whether the solar disposal creates any FY27 gain/loss on disposal or changes electricity cost lines.

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

- **Note 4 footnote [^1] — "Based on customer country of origin." [D006.3, p. 25]**
  - This qualifies the geographical revenue split (South Africa 1,023,299; Australia 132,804; UK/Europe 328; US 20). It means the split reflects **where the customer is domiciled, not where the mine is located**. Implication for forecasting: the Australia 132,804 line is a customer-market attribution (likely Tennant offtake to an Australian refiner/buyer), not a proxy for Australian asset revenue. Do not use this table to allocate revenue by producing asset — use D006.2 segment analysis for that. Verification: cross-check Australia 132,804 against Tennant segment revenue in D006.2 and D009.

- **Contract liability narrative (unfootnoted but commentary-bearing) [D006.3, p. 25]**
  - Two FY26 advance receipts totalling 8,030 (Barberton RMB forward US$4.5m + Tennant US$3.5m), **both settled in physical bullion in July 2026** (i.e., FY27). This is a critical phasing point: the cash was received in FY26 but the revenue/ounces will be recognised in FY27. Forecast implication: FY27 gold revenue includes ~8,030 of ounces delivered against FY26 prepayments with **no matching FY27 cash inflow** — a working-capital drag in FY27 and a reason FY27 revenue may exceed FY27 cash receipts. Also confirms the prior 24-month RMB forward (4,846oz/month @ ZAR1,025,000/kg ≈ US$1,723/oz) was fully fulfilled in FY25 — no residual hedged ounces overhang into FY27. Verification: check D009/D008 that July 2026 deliveries are counted in FY27 ounces and that no double-count occurs against FY27 production guidance.

- **Note 5 — "Borrowing costs capitalised – FY26 nil vs FY25 7,190" [D006.4, p. 25]**
  - This is a material qualification on the borrowings cost line: FY25 gross borrowing cost was effectively 25,033 − 7,190 = 17,843 net of capitalisation; FY26 is 14,505 with **no capitalisation**. The 42% YoY fall in the borrowings line (25,033→14,505) is therefore partly a capitalisation-cessation effect, not purely deleveraging. Forecast implication: FY27 finance cost run-rate should be anchored on the FY26 14,505 (no capitalisation assumed) unless new project capex (Tennant crusher/mill/filter, Soweto Cluster) triggers renewed capitalisation. Verification: check D003/D008 for any FY27 capitalised interest guidance and debt balances.

- **Note 5 — Environmental rehabilitation obligation finance cost (2,025) [D006.4, p. 25]**
  - Non-cash accretion; stable YoY (2,156→2,025). Retain as a recurring non-cash finance cost in FY27 model; do not treat as cash interest.

- **Note 5 — "Fair value gain on short-term investments 1,142 (Note 13)" [D006.4, p. 25]**
  - Confirms STI are FVTPL and the gain is non-cash/volatile. Forecast implication: do not annualise the 1,142 as recurring finance income; treat as mark-to-market. Verification: Note 13 (not in this capture) for STI balance and nature.

### [SECTION READING RESPONSE / DETERMINATION]
### 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

**Q26 (remainder) / Q32 / Q39 — Deferred tax liability +110m drivers; FY27 expected effective tax rate**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D008/D009)`
- **Answer & evidence**: The +US$109,974k movement in net DTL (140,506 → 250,480) is driven almost entirely by **PPE temporary differences: 136,460 → 255,589 (+US$119,129k)** [D006.4, p.26]. The reconciliation confirms: opening 140,506 + DTL recognised in P&L 97,377 + FX translation 13,083 − transferred from DTA 486 = 250,480 [D006.4, p.26]. So the DTL build is (a) P&L deferred tax charge of ~97.4m (vs 36.3m in FY25 — a 2.7x step-up) and (b) a US$13.1m FX translation reserve movement (ZAR/AUD strengthening on ZAR/AUD-denominated DTLs). The PPE driver is consistent with the Australia non-current asset step-up (+US$209m per prior ledger) and accelerated capitalisation. **FY27 expected ETR is NOT stated in this section** — the deferred tax note gives rates applied (Barberton 28.29%, Evander 29.03%, MTR 29.95%, Tennant 30.00%, Other 27.00%) but no forward ETR guidance. Remainder stays open for D008 (APM/tax reconciliation) and D009.

**Q49 — Note 13 STI balance / FVTPL classification / 1,142 FV gain recurring**
- **Status**: `Not resolved in this section`
- **Answer & evidence**: This section (Note 6 Income Tax) does not contain Note 13 or the STI balance. The only tangential evidence is a **"Fair value adjustment on investment" DTL of 569 (FY26) vs nil (FY25)** [D006.4, p.26], which implies a fair-value gain on an investment was recognised in FY26 and taxed — consistent with (but not confirming) the 1,142 FV gain referenced in the stored question. Carry forward to D008/D009.

**Q50 — Restricted cash 2,612 / finance income 60 FY26 vs nil FY25**
- **Status**: `Not resolved in this section`
- **Answer & evidence**: Note 6 does not address restricted cash or finance income. Carry forward.

**Q51 — Other receivables 41,654 composition**
- **Status**: `Not resolved in this section`
- **Answer & evidence**: Note 6 does not address other receivables. Carry forward.

**Q24, Q25, Q27, Q28, Q29, Q30, Q31, Q33, Q35, Q38, Q43, Q44** — none of these are addressed by Note 6 evidence; carry forward unchanged.

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

**Footnote [^1] — p.26 (DTL table and DTA table)**
- **Quote**: *"In the previous reporting period, employee obligations, cash-settled share-based payment obligations and the expected credit loss allowance were incorrectly classified as an environmental rehabilitation obligation. These items have been disaggregated in the current reporting period, with comparative figures restated accordingly."* [D006.4, p.26]
- **What it changes**: The FY25 comparative DTL line items are **restated**, not as originally reported. This means any FY25 DTL build analysis using previously published figures will not tie. Critically, the restatement **removes an "environmental rehabilitation obligation" DTL line entirely** — the current table has no such line. This raises a verification question: where is the rehabilitation provision DTL now carried (is it inside "Other" at (81), or was it reclassified out)? The restated FY25 employee obligations DTL is (2,283), cash-settled SBP (1,768), ECL (48) — all small. The restatement is presentational, not a change in total net DTL (140,506 unchanged), but it obscures the rehabilitation DTL quantum.
- **Must verify in later sections**: Whether the rehabilitation provision deferred tax is disclosed elsewhere (provisions note) and whether it is material to the FY27 DTL run-rate.

**Footnote [^1] — p.26 (DTA table)**
- **Quote**: *"Other payables previously included the temporary difference arising from the accrual for employee benefits and leave pay liability. This amount has been disaggregated in the current reporting period, with comparative figures restated accordingly."* [D006.4, p.26]
- **What it changes**: Same restatement theme — FY25 DTA "Other payables" (545) is restated and a new "Employee obligations" DTA line (626) appears. Small magnitudes; no forecast impact beyond confirming comparatives are not clean.

**Unnumbered qualification — p.26 (DTA recognition)**
- **Quote**: *"Deferred tax assets have only been recognised, where applicable, on the basis that the individual Group companies will be able to generate future taxable economic benefits to utilise current deductible temporary differences."* [D006.4, p.26]
- **What it changes**: Confirms DTA recognition is entity-by-entity and contingent on future taxable profits. Net DTA is only 1,921 — immaterial. But it signals that **assessed losses dropped from 6,729 (FY25) to 3 (FY26)** — i.e., the group has largely consumed its tax losses, which is a key reason the **current tax charge jumped from 19,348 to 73,486 (+280%)** and the **effective tax rate rose**. This is a structural step-up, not a one-off: FY27 current tax should be modelled on the higher, loss-depleted base.

**Footnote [^2] marker on "Other payables" DTA line (91)** — the footnote text for [^2] is **NOT present in this section capture**. The [^1] footnote appears twice but there is no [^2] definition printed. This is a **missing footnote** — flag as a verification question (consistent with the pre-existing Q27/Q35 numbering-gap concern).

**Arithmetic checks performed:**
- DTL reconciliation: 140,506 + 97,377 + 13,083 − 486 = **250,480** ✓ ties.
- DTA reconciliation: 2,072 + 205 − 486 + 130 = **1,921** ✓ ties.
- P&L deferred tax per 6.1 = 97,172; per DTL reconciliation = 97,377; per DTA reconciliation = 205. Net = 97,377 − 205 = **97,172** ✓ ties to 6.1.
- Current tax: SA 73,486 + Aus 6,799 = **80,285**; + deferred 97,172 = **177,457** ✓ ties to total income tax expense.
- **Tension flagged**: FY26 ETR = 177,457 / pre-tax profit. Pre-tax profit not in this section, but prior ledger noted FY26 ETR 33.2% vs FY25 28.5%. The jump is explained by (i) loss depletion (assessed loss DTA 6,729→3) and (ii) higher deferred tax on PPE. Verify pre-tax profit in D005.1 to confirm ETR.

[D006.5, p.28]

**Q38 — Extract Australian PPE depreciation run-rate and Emmerson asset FV; confirm FY27 D&A step-up.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (Emmerson FV partially; Australian depreciation split not isolated)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Total FY26 depreciation on PPE = **US$61,733k** vs FY25 **US$34,394k** — a **+79% step-up**, consistent with a full year of Tennant + ~1 week of Emmerson. Emmerson additions through acquisition = **US$142,436k** (of which mineral rights/exploration US$142,244k; ROU assets US$112k; other US$80k) — this is the FV of Emmerson PPE acquired, not the full consideration. The note does **not** split Australian vs South African depreciation; that split must come from D006.2 segment note or D008. FY27 D&A will step up further on a full year of Emmerson + Tennant capex. [D006.5, p.27–28]

These are the gross capex drivers behind the deferred tax movement; the DTL reconciliation itself sits in Note 6 (D006.4). [D006.5, p.27]

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

- **`[^1]` on "Additions through acquisitions" (US$142,436k)** — `[D006.5, p.27]`. The footnote text reads only "Refer to note 18." **Note 18 is not in this capture.** This is the single most consequential footnote in the section: it is the gateway to the Emmerson business-combination disclosure (consideration, FV of net assets, goodwill vs bargain purchase, and whether any of the US$142,244k mineral rights is a provisional PPA allocation subject to remeasurement within 12 months). **Implication**: the US$142,436k is a *provisional* FV that could be revised; if a bargain purchase gain exists it would flow through P&L and distort FY26 earnings and the FY27 base. **Verification required**: locate Note 18 in a later capture (D006.6+ or D007) to confirm consideration, goodwill/bargain, and whether the PPA is final or provisional.

- **"Derecognition" of US$4,301k cost / US$3,725k accumulated depreciation (net ~US$576k)** — `[D006.5, p.27–28]`. No footnote explains what was derecognised. The ROU buildings/infrastructure line shows a US$2,953k cost derecognition offset by US$3,094k accumulated depreciation reversal — a **net credit** to the ROU asset. This looks like a lease termination/modification. **Implication**: check whether this is a one-off lease exit (e.g., solar plant disposal referenced in Q40) that changes the FY27 ROU depreciation and lease liability run-rate. **Verification required**: confirm nature of derecognition in the leases note (not captured).

- **"Transfers" net US$2,584k cost / net −US$2,661k accumulated depreciation** — `[D006.5, p.27–28]`. Large gross transfers (e.g., US$61,388k into plant & machinery owned, US$59,795k out of capital under construction) confirm **capital under construction is being commissioned into depreciable plant**. **Implication**: this is the mechanism by which FY27 depreciation steps up — assets moving from non-depreciating CUWIP (US$234,145k carrying at 30 Jun 2026) into depreciable plant. **Verification required**: confirm in D008/D009 the commissioning schedule of the US$234m CUWIP (Tennant crusher/mill/filter, White Devil) to time the FY27 D&A step-up.

- **"Increase in environmental rehabilitation obligation" US$6,520k added to PPE cost** — `[D006.5, p.27]`. This is an asset-retirement-cost capitalisation (mineral rights US$5,042k + buildings US$1,305k + exploration US$173k). **Implication**: it inflates PPE and the rehabilitation provision simultaneously; the related deferred tax was flagged as removed from the DTL table in Q55. **Verification required**: confirm the rehabilitation provision balance and its deferred tax treatment in the provisions note (not captured).

- **Depreciation reconciliation to cost of production** — `[D006.5, p.28]`. Total D&A in cost of production = **US$60,288k** (FY26) vs **US$33,360k** (FY25). Note the **"Add back: other depreciation and amortisation" of US$2,179k** — this is a reconciling item that removes non-production D&A from the cost-of-production figure. **Implication**: for AISC/unit-cost modelling, use US$60,288k as the production D&A, not the US$61,733k total PPE depreciation. The US$2,179k add-back is the D&A sitting outside cost of production (e.g., corporate/exploration). **Verification required**: confirm in D008 that AISC uses the US$60,288k figure.

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

**Note 8 — Capital Expenditure (no footnotes, but structural observations):**
- No footnote markers in Note 8. However, the table itself carries forecast-relevant structure: FY26 total capital US$223,995k vs FY25 US$167,909k (+33%), driven by expansion capital US$202,163k vs US$156,283k. Sustaining capital nearly doubled (21,832 vs 11,626). [D006.6, p.29]
- **Forecast implication**: FY26 capex is a heavy year; the split by asset (Tennant 71,304; Evander 49,822; Barberton 40,081; MTR 32,599; Elikhulu 21,119; Solar 7,159; Corporate 1,833; Agri 78) must be checked against FY27 guidance in D008/D009 to determine whether FY27 capex steps down (MTR FY25 52,206 → FY26 32,599 already declining; Tennant still rising). [D006.6, p.29]
- **Verification needed**: FY27 capex guidance by asset, and whether Tennant expansion capital (68,436) is completing or continuing into FY27 (links Q37, Q60). [D006.6, p.29]

**Note 9 — Investments, footnote [^1]:**
- Footnote [^1] states: "CuFe is an ASX-listed emerging copper, gold and iron ore exploration and development company. Its flagship asset is the Gecko/Orlando project in the Tennant Creek region of the Northern Territory... Tennant company acquired 307,039,759 of CuFe's issued share capital on 19 May 2026 representing a 15% shareholding." [D006.6, p.29]
- **What it changes**: The US$11,265k "Acquisition of investment in CuFe" is a 15% strategic stake in a Tennant Creek neighbour — not a controlled subsidiary. It is carried at FV through OCI (FV adjustment 1,897 through OCI, not P&L). This means: (i) no consolidation of CuFe; (ii) the 1,897 FV gain is OCI, not earnings — do not model in P&L; (iii) the 15% stake may signal future M&A/regional consolidation around Tennant Creek. [D006.6, p.29]
- **Verification needed**: Confirm CuFe is equity-accounted or FVOCI (the separate "Investment in associate 86" line suggests a different, small associate); confirm whether the 15% stake triggers any equity-accounting or related-party disclosure; check Note 18/segment note for Tennant Creek regional strategy. [D006.6, p.29]

**Note 10 — Inventory, footnote [^1]:**
- Footnote [^1]: "The long-term inventory relates to a holding of tailings contained in Barberton Mines' Harper TSF, Mogale Gold, MSC and Tennant Mines." Plus: "There was no write-down of inventory to net realisable value or any reversal of write-downs in the current or previous reporting period." [D006.6, p.29]
- **What it changes**: Long-term inventory fell sharply to 11,911 from 25,698 (−13,787), while current portion of long-term inventory fell to 9,788 from 18,131 (−8,343). Total inventory down to 50,960 from 64,585. This is a material drawdown of tailings/ore stockpiles — consistent with higher production/feed in FY26. The "no NRV write-down" statement is a positive quality signal (no hidden impairment). [D006.6, p.29]
- **Forecast implication**: The tailings drawdown (Harper TSF, Mogale, MSC, Tennant) is a non-renewable feed source; if FY26 production was boosted by destocking, FY27 may face a feed headwind unless replaced by new ore. Must check D009 production summary and D008 for whether FY27 guidance assumes continued tailings drawdown. [D006.6, p.29]
- **Verification needed**: Confirm FY27 tailings/stockpile feed assumption; reconcile inventory drawdown to cost of production (inventory recognised in cost of production rose to 57,468 from 27,358 — a US$30,110k step-up, which is a large swing; check whether this reflects higher sales volumes or reclassification). [D006.6, p.29]

**Note 11 — Other Receivables, footnotes [^1], [^2], [^3]:**
- [^1]: "Other receivables arise from transactions outside the normal operating activities... mainly a large number of small debtor balances of US$4.5 million (FY25: US$2.4 million) of Evander Mines and Barberton Mines." [D006.6, p.29]
- [^2]: "The year-on-year increase is due to prepayments made to secure cyanide supply amid the ongoing geopolitical disruptions." [D006.6, p.29]
- [^3]: "Indirect taxes receivable comprises value-added tax (VAT) for the South African operations and goods and services tax (GST) for the Australian operations." [D006.6, p.29]
- **What it changes**: The 41,654 other receivables is dominated by non-financial items (34,021 = 82%): prepayments 15,914 (cyanide security — a working-capital and supply-risk signal) and indirect taxes 18,107 (VAT/GST — recoverable but a cash timing drag). The cyanide prepayment footnote is a genuine operational caveat: geopolitical disruption to cyanide supply is forcing cash prepayment, which (i) ties up working capital, (ii) may signal supply-cost inflation risk into FY27. [D006.6, p.29]
- **Forecast implication**: Model prepayments as elevated working capital in FY27 unless supply normalises; watch cyanide cost per tonne in D008/D009. Indirect taxes receivable 18,107 is a cash recovery timing item — check cash flow statement (D005) for whether it reversed post-year-end. [D006.6, p.29]
- **Verification needed**: Confirm whether cyanide prepayments are expected to unwind in FY27 (working capital release) or persist; confirm VAT/GST receivable recoverability and timing. [D006.6, p.29]

**Arithmetic checks performed:**
- Note 8: FY26 total 223,995 = 21,832 + 202,163 ✓. FY25 total 167,909 = 11,626 + 156,283 ✓. Asset sum FY26: 40,081+49,822+21,119+32,599+71,304+1,833+78+7,159 = 223,995 ✓. [D006.6, p.29]
- Note 9: 12,705 + 86 = 12,791 ✓. Movement: 0 + 11,265 + 1,897 − 457 = 12,705 ✓. [D006.6, p.29]
- Note 10: Current inventory 39,049 = 9,162+20,592+9,788−493 ✓. Total 50,960 = 39,049+11,911 ✓. [D006.6, p.29]
- Note 11: 7,633 + 34,021 = 41,654 ✓. 7,828 − 195 = 7,633 ✓. 15,914 + 18,107 = 34,021 ✓. [D006.6, p.29]
- **Tension flagged**: Note 10 "Inventory recognised in cost of production" 57,468 vs FY25 27,358 — a US$30,110k increase (+110%) that is not explained by any footnote. This is a large swing that needs reconciliation to cost of sales / production volumes in D005/D008. [D006.6, p.29]

### 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

- **Q49: Extract Note 13 STI balance; confirm FVTPL; whether 1,142 FV gain recurring (distinct from CuFe 1,897 OCI mark).**
  - **Status**: `Resolved`
  - **Answer & evidence**: Note 13 confirms the short-term investments (STI) balance at FY26 is **US$137,867k** (FY25: nil), comprising money market funds of US$18,573k and core income funds of US$119,294k. The note explicitly states these are classified as **financial assets at fair value through profit or loss (FVTPL)**. The FV gain recognised in P&L is **US$1,142k** (cross-referenced to Note 5), which is distinct from the CuFe OCI mark of US$1,897k captured earlier. The gain is a mark-to-market on treasury funds and is not a recurring operating item; it should be treated as a below-the-line/non-operating item in the forecast. [D006.7, physical PDF p. 30]

- **Q23 (remainder): Confirm Emmerson consideration split, FV net assets, goodwill vs bargain gain.**
  - **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D006.5)`
  - **Answer & evidence**: The share consideration is confirmed (102,641,421 shares at US$1.44 = ~US$147.8m), but the FV net assets and goodwill vs bargain gain require Note 18. [D006.7, physical PDF p. 30]

- **Q41: Confirm Emmerson consideration, FV net assets, goodwill vs bargain, FY27 exploration/royalty contribution.**
  - **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D006.5)`
  - **Answer & evidence**: Consideration confirmed as above; FV net assets, goodwill/bargain, and FY27 contribution require Note 18. [D006.7, physical PDF p. 30]

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

- **Note 12, footnote [^1] — Other payables commentary** [D006.7, physical PDF p. 30]:
  - **Quote**: "Other payables comprise various short-term liabilities not classified as trade payables or borrowings. These include accrued expenses for utilities and professional fees among other operational accruals. The year-on-year increase is primarily as a result of the commissioning of Tennant Mines and the processing of third-party gold-bearing material."
  - **What it changes**: Other payables increased from US$20,825k to US$50,305k (+US$29,480k, +141.6%). The footnote attributes this to Tennant commissioning and third-party ore processing. This is a **working capital build** that should be modelled as a source of cash in FY26 but may reverse or stabilise in FY27 as commissioning completes. It also signals that Tennant's third-party processing activity is generating operational accruals that may not recur at the same rate.
  - **Verification required**: Confirm in D009/D008 whether third-party processing volumes are expected to continue in FY27 and whether the accrued utilities/professional fees will unwind.

- **Note 12 — VAT payable** [D006.7, physical PDF p. 30]:
  - VAT payable increased from US$21k to US$5,836k (+US$5,815k). This is a large proportional increase and may relate to Tennant/Australian GST or Emmerson. It should be checked against the cash flow statement for timing of payment.

- **Note 13 — Short-term investments qualification** [D006.7, physical PDF p. 30]:
  - **Quote**: "These short-term investments are held as part of the Group's treasury and liquidity management activities and are highly liquid and readily available for withdrawal. Notwithstanding their short-term nature and high liquidity, the investments do not qualify as cash and cash equivalents and are classified as financial assets at fair value through profit or loss."
  - **What it changes**: The US$137,867k STI balance is **excluded from cash and cash equivalents** but is highly liquid. For forecasting, this should be treated as near-cash for liquidity purposes but **below the EBITDA line** for FV gains (US$1,142k). The FV gain is non-recurring and should be stripped out of operating earnings.
  - **Verification required**: Confirm in D005.1 whether the STI is included in the "cash + STI US$246.2m" figure and how it reconciles to the cash flow statement.

- **Note 14.1, footnote [^1] — Prior period share issues** [D006.7, physical PDF p. 30]:
  - **Quote**: "During the previous reporting period, 83,597,210 shares were issued as consideration for the acquisition of Tennant company and 4,298,400 were issued as consideration for the acquisition of Yungatha. The remaining 24,917,607 shares were issued to a former lender of Tennant company, in settlement of an existing debt obligation."
  - **What it changes**: Confirms FY25 share issuance of 112,813,218 shares was for acquisitions (Tennant, Yungatha) and debt settlement. This is historical and does not affect FY27 forecast except for the diluted share count. The debt settlement (24,917,607 shares) implies a non-cash financing transaction that should be checked against the cash flow statement.

- **Note 14.1, footnote [^2] — Share buy-back** [D006.7, physical PDF p. 30]:
  - **Quote**: "The Company completed a share buy-back programme during the current reporting period, which resulted in the total shares of the Company decreasing by 2,003,735 at an average price of 47.8 pence (US$0.66 cents) per share."
  - **What it changes**: The buy-back cost approximately US$1,322k (2,003,735 × US$0.66). This is a cash outflow that should be reflected in the cash flow statement. The buy-back reduces the share count marginally and may signal a capital return policy. The "US$0.66 cents" wording is ambiguous (likely US$0.66, not 0.66 cents) and should be verified.
  - **Verification required**: Confirm the total buy-back cash cost in D005.1 and whether further buy-backs are planned for FY27.

- **Note 14.2 — Shares to be issued** [D006.7, physical PDF p. 30]:
  - **Quote**: "Pursuant to the Emmerson acquisition becoming effective on 22 June 2026, the Company is required to issue 102,641,421 new Company ordinary shares to eligible Emmerson shareholders on 1 July 2026 in settlement for the acquired Emmerson shares. The new ordinary shares will be credited as fully paid at GBP1.09 (US$1.44) per share; refer to note 18.1."
  - **What it changes**: This confirms the Emmerson consideration is **share-based** (no cash outflow). The shares are issued **post-year-end** (1 July 2026), so they are not in the FY26 issued share count (2,333,671,529) but will increase FY27 issued shares to approximately 2,436,312,950 (2,333,671,529 + 102,641,421). This is critical for FY27 EPS, dividend per share, and weighted average share count. The US$1.44 share price implies a consideration of ~US$147.8m.
  - **Verification required**: Confirm in Note 18.1 the total consideration, FV net assets, and goodwill/bargain purchase gain. Confirm the FY27 weighted average share count including these shares.

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

**Note 15, footnote [^1] — Tennant lender shares** [D006.8, p.31]:
> "During the previous reporting period, 24,917,607 shares were issued to a prior lender of Tennant company in settlement of an existing debt obligation."

- **What it changes**: Confirms the FY25 share premium movement of US$10,877k was a **non-cash debt-for-equity settlement**, not a cash raise. This means FY25 "Shares issued" of US$10,877k should be excluded from any free cash flow or equity raise analysis. It also confirms the Tennant debt settlement was completed in FY25, with no residual in FY26 borrowings.
- **Verification needed**: Confirm in Note 18 (Emmerson) whether any similar share-based consideration exists, and confirm the 24,917,607 shares are included in the FY26 weighted average share count (they were issued in FY25, so should be in both FY25 and FY26 weighted counts).

**Note 15, "Capital reduction" commentary** [D006.8, p.31]:
> "Formal approval of the capital reduction in the current reporting period was granted by the High Court... on 28 April 2025. The Court order... was registered with the Registrar of Companies on 21 May 2026, and therefore the capital reduction became effective on this date. Following the share capital reduction, the Company's share premium account was cancelled in full, with the amount appropriated to retained earnings."

- **What it changes**: The FY26 capital reduction of US$10,877k is a **non-cash reserve reclassification** (share premium → retained earnings), not a distribution. It creates distributable reserves, which is a **precondition for dividend payments**. This supports the FY26 dividend but does not itself represent a cash outflow. The FY25 capital reduction (US$235,063k) was similarly non-cash.
- **Forecast implication**: No P&L or cash flow impact. But it confirms the company now has distributable reserves to support the FY26 dividend and potentially FY27 dividends.
- **Verification needed**: Confirm in D005.1 (Statement of Changes in Equity) that the US$10,877k reclassification appears correctly and that retained earnings increased by that amount.

**Note 16.1 — RCF amendment and JIBAR→ZARONIA transition** [D006.8, p.31]:
> "The amendment extended the maturity date of the existing RCF of ZAR1 billion (US$54.1 million) to 30 June 2027."
> "Addenda to the relevant facility agreements were signed on 12 August 2026. The transition will take effect on the next interest rate reset rollover date of 29 September 2026. The change is not expected to result in a substantial modification under IFRS 9."

- **What it changes**: The RCF maturity is **30 June 2027** — i.e., it matures within FY27. This is a **near-term refinancing risk** that must be flagged. The ZAR1bn facility is only US$60k drawn at FY26 year-end, so the refinancing risk is about **availability**, not repayment. The JIBAR→ZARONIA transition is post-year-end (12 Aug 2026 addenda, effective 29 Sep 2026) and is not expected to be a substantial modification — so no P&L impact expected, but the **interest rate basis changes** from JIBAR to ZARONIA, which may alter the effective rate.
- **Verification needed**: Confirm in D008 (APM) or D003 (CEO statement) whether the RCF is expected to be renewed before 30 June 2027, and whether the ZARONIA transition changes the effective interest rate materially.

**Note 16.2 — Term loan settled** [D006.8, p.31]:
> "The term loan was settled during the current reporting period and the facility is no longer available."

- **What it changes**: The term loan (FY25: US$68,804k) was **fully repaid** in FY26 (repayment of capital US$73,025k). This is a **major debt reduction** — FY27 interest savings should be material. The facility is **no longer available**, so the group cannot redraw it. This reduces financial flexibility.
- **Forecast implication**: FY27 finance costs should drop by approximately the term loan's FY26 finance cost of US$2,860k (plus unwinding of fees US$813k). The US$73,025k repayment is a **cash outflow** in FY26 financing activities — confirm in D005.1 cash flow statement.

**Note 16.3 — DMTN bonds** [D006.8, p.32]:
> "PARS01 was fully settled and PARS03 was partially settled during the current reporting period."
> "The Group will proceed with amending and restating its applicable pricing supplements applicable to the DMTN bonds once legislation has been enacted for PARS02 and PARS03 regarding the transition from JIBAR to ZARONIA."

- **What it changes**: DMTN bonds reduced from US$67,972k to US$51,475k (settlement of US$20,990k). The **PARS02 and PARS03 JIBAR→ZARONIA transition is pending legislation** — this is a **future interest rate risk** that is not yet quantified. The current portion is US$12,896k (due within FY27), non-current US$38,579k.
- **Forecast implication**: FY27 DMTN finance cost run-rate ≈ US$6,364k (FY26 incurred) less the settled PARS01 portion. Need the coupon rates on PARS02/PARS03 to model accurately.
- **Verification needed**: Confirm in D008 or D005.1 the DMTN coupon rates and maturity profile for PARS02/PARS03.

**Note 16.4 — Realside facility settled 26 Feb 2026** [D006.8, p.32]:
- **What it changes**: US$31,856k capital repaid + US$2,890k finance costs repaid = **US$34,746k cash outflow** in FY26. This was an Australia-related facility (from the Tennant acquisition). Its settlement removes US$2,952k of FY26 finance costs from the FY27 run-rate.

**Note 16.5 — NT Australia loan settled 27 Feb 2026** [D006.8, p.32]:
- **What it changes**: US$7,094k capital + US$769k finance costs repaid = **US$7,863k cash outflow**. Removes US$274k FY26 finance costs from FY27 run-rate.

**Note 16.6 — NAB loan settled 30 June 2026** [D006.8, p.32]:
- **What it changes**: US$2,412k capital + US$133k finance costs repaid = **US$2,545k cash outflow**. Removes US$99k FY26 finance costs from FY27 run-rate.

**Note 16.7, footnote [^1] — Guarantees** [D006.8, p.33]:
> "The guarantees issued to Eskom Holdings SOC Limited relate to the supply of electricity. RMB issued a guarantee to Eskom on behalf of MTR resulting in an increase in the Eskom guarantee. The guarantees issued to the DMPR relate to the Group's environmental rehabilitation obligation."

- **What it changes**: The Eskom guarantee increased from US$3,761k to US$5,271k (+US$1,510k) — this is a **contingent liability** related to MTR electricity supply. The DMPR guarantee of US$40,575k (up from US$37,626k) is the **environmental rehabilitation obligation** — this is a real future cash cost, not just a guarantee. The increase of US$2,949k may reflect updated rehabilitation estimates.
- **Forecast implication**: The DMPR rehabilitation obligation is a **long-term liability** that should be modelled as a future cash outflow. The Eskom guarantee is a contingent liability — no cash impact unless called.
- **Verification needed**: Confirm in D006.5 (PPE) or D006.6 (provisions) the rehabilitation provision balance and whether the US$40,575k guarantee matches the provision.

**Note 16.7, footnote [^2] — General banking facility** [D006.8, p.33]:
> "The Nedbank Limited and RMB general banking facilities are unsecured and were unutilised in the current and previous reporting periods. These facilities, when utilised, bear interest at rates linked to the South African prime interest rate."

- **What it changes**: US$18,226k of undrawn, unsecured general banking facilities are **available liquidity** but not drawn. This is a **liquidity buffer** for FY27. The prime-linked rate means if drawn, cost would be prime + margin.

**Note 16.7, footnote [^3] — Gold and derivative trading facilities** [D006.8, p.33]:
> "The US$ gold and derivative trading facilities are used by the Group for the purpose of trading gold inventory and subsequent conversion of US$ sales proceeds into ZAR. The facilities are held at Absa Bank Limited, Nedbank Limited, Rand Merchant Bank Limited and Investec Bank Limited."

- **What it changes**: US$58,931k of gold trading facilities (up from US$35,003k) — this is a **significant increase** (+US$23,928k). These are used for **gold inventory trading and FX conversion**, not for debt funding. The increase may reflect higher gold prices (more value per ounce traded) or increased trading volumes. This is **not debt** and should not be included in net debt calculations.
- **Forecast implication**: The gold loan facility (US$25,557k, up from US$16,338k) is a **gold borrowing facility** — if drawn, it represents a gold price exposure. The increase of US$9,219k may indicate increased gold borrowing, which would be a **hedging/price exposure** item.
- **Verification needed**: Confirm in D008 (APM) whether the gold loan facility is included in net debt or treated as a trading facility. Confirm in D009 (production summary) whether gold borrowing affects reported sales volumes.
