18. ACQUISITIONS AND DISPOSALS
18.1 Acquisition of Emmerson Resources Limited (Emmerson)
The Group, through its wholly owned subsidiary Tennant company, acquired 100% of the issued shares 
in Emmerson by way of an Australian Court-approved scheme of arrangement ( scheme) in accordance 
with Part 5.1 of the Australian Corporations Act 2001. Tennant company obtained control of Emmerson 
on 22 June 2026 as the scheme came into effect and was legally enforceable and binding on all parties on 
22 June 2026, as determined by section 411(10) of the Corporations Act 2001.
The acquisition represents Pan African’s next step since acquiring Tennant company in FY25 and consolidates 
its position in the TCMF. In addition, Emmerson has been a joint venture partner since establishing the Tennant 
Creek Joint Venture in 2020. Bringing the assets under single ownership allows Pan African to optimise project 
sequencing and capital allocation across the region, effectively facilitating economies of scale by eliminating the 
complexity of joint venture arrangements, and positions Pan African to capture the full value of Tennant Creek 
through consolidation, ultimately maximising value for all shareholders. 
Under the terms of the scheme, Emmerson shareholders were entitled to receive 0.1493 new Pan African shares, 
in the form of ASX-listed Pan African CDIs, for each Emmerson share held. The scheme was implemented and 
Pan African shares were allotted to the Emmerson shareholders on 1 July 2026.
Furthermore, in conjunction with the scheme, Pan African listed on the ASX by way of a Foreign Exempt Listing, 
providing Emmerson shareholders with the ability to trade Pan African CDIs on the ASX. Pan African’s shares 
continue to trade, as a dual primary issuer, on the LSE and JSE following the ASX listing.
The purchase price, determined by way of a fixed exchange rate, was settled on 1 July 2026. 
102,641,421 Pan African shares were issued at GBP1.09 (US$1.44) per share resulting in a purchase price of 
US$148.2 million. As control arose on 22 June 2026 and the purchase price settled on 1 July 2026, being after 
the reporting date, a reserve for shares to be issued was recognised as at 30 June 2026; refer to note 14.
While Tennant company obtained control of Emmerson on 22 June 2026, for practical purposes and given that 
there were no significant transactions or movements in fair values between 22 June 2026 and 30 June 2026, 
Emmerson has been consolidated from 30 June 2026.
Included in the identifiable assets acquired are inputs in the form of mineral rights. However, no active operations, 
assets or a skilled workforce to extract gold or generate income from ordinary activities were identified on the 
date of acquisition. The acquisition of Emmerson, therefore, did not constitute the acquisition of a business as 
there was no integrated set of activities in place capable of being managed to convert the acquired inputs into 
outputs. As such, the acquisition was recognised as an asset acquisition.
 On acquisition, Tennant company was required to identify and recognise the individual assets and liabilities 
acquired. The purchase price was allocated to the assets acquired and liabilities assumed based on their fair 
values at the date of acquisition, resulting in the excess purchase consideration over net identifiable asset 
value being allocated to the exploration assets. This allocation reflects the concentration of value in the mineral 
extraction and exploration rights acquired. Since the transaction did not constitute the acquisition of a business, 
no goodwill was recognised

### 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

**Q59 / Q76 / Q91 — Locate Note 18 (Emmerson): total consideration, FV net assets, goodwill vs bargain purchase gain, PPA provisional or final**
- **Status**: `Resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: Note 18.1 is on physical PDF p. 34. Total cost of acquisition = **US$160,097k**, comprising ordinary shares to be issued **102,641,421 at GBP1.09 = US$148,218k** plus directly attributable transaction costs capitalised **US$11,879k**. Allocation to net assets acquired totals **US$160,097k** (PP&E 142,436 — of which exploration assets 142,244, leased buildings 112, other 80; investment 86; trade & other receivables 6,427; cash 12,796; lease liability (133); trade & other payables (1,515)). **No goodwill and no bargain purchase gain** — the acquisition was accounted for as an **asset acquisition** (not a business combination) because no integrated set of activities/skilled workforce existed; the excess of consideration over net identifiable assets was allocated to exploration assets. There is **no PPA** in the IFRS 3 sense; the allocation is a purchase-price allocation to individual assets/liabilities at fair value. `[D006.9, physical PDF p. 34]`

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

- **No footnote markers (`[^1]`, `*`) appear in the supplied D006.9 evidence.** All qualifications are embedded in body text rather than footnotes. Key embedded qualifications:
  1. **"Consolidated from 30 June 2026" vs control date 22 June 2026** `[D006.9, p. 34]`: This is a material phasing caveat. Emmerson contributes **effectively zero** to FY26 P&L (only balance-sheet consolidation at 30 June). For forecasting, **all Emmerson operating/exploration activity falls into FY27 onward** — do not expect any FY26 revenue/ounce contribution. Verify in D009 whether any Emmerson ounces are included in FY26 group production (they should not be).
  2. **"Asset acquisition, not a business combination — no goodwill"** `[D006.9, p. 34]`: Changes interpretation of the US$142,244k "exploration assets" — this is capitalised mineral rights value, **not** goodwill. For forecast: this sits in PPE/exploration assets and may attract **depletion/amortisation or impairment testing** in FY27; confirm treatment in D006.5 (Note 7) and D008.
  3. **"Purchase price settled 1 July 2026; reserve for shares to be issued at 30 June 2026"** `[D006.9, p. 34]`: The 102,641,421 shares are **not in the 30 June 2026 issued share count** but **are in the FY27 weighted average count from 1 July 2026**. This directly affects Q77 (FY27 WAS) and Q3 (dividend implied share counts). Confirm in D005.1/D008.
  4. **"Directly attributable transaction costs capitalised US$11,879k"** `[D006.9, p. 34]`: These are **capitalised, not expensed** — so they do **not** hit FY26 P&L/Adjusted EBITDA. For forecast, ensure no double-count of transaction costs in FY27 opex; confirm in D008 whether any are excluded from APMs.
  5. **"No disposals during current or previous reporting period"** `[D006.9, p. 34]` (Note 18.2): Contradicts/qualifies any earlier expectation of a **solar project disposal** (Q40/Q72). If solar assets were to be disposed, Note 18.2 says none occurred in FY26 — so the solar disposal (if any) must be **FY27+ or was not a disposal**. Flag for D008/D003 verification.

  20.2 Fair value information                          
     Fair value of financial instruments               
     The directors consider the carrying amounts of financial assets and liabilities to approximate their fair values due
     to their short-term nature.                       
     Fair value hierarchy                              
     Financial instruments measured at fair value are classified in the fair value hierarchy based on the extent to which
     fair value is observable.                         
     The levels are determined as follows:             
     Level 1 – Fair value is based on quoted prices in active markets for identical financial assets or liabilities.
     Level 2 – Fair value is determined using inputs other than quoted prices included within Level 1 that are
         observable for the asset or liability, either directly (i.e. prices) or indirectly (i.e. derived from prices).
     Level 3 – Fair value is determined on inputs not based on observable market data.

Power purchase agreement
The Company entered into a PPA on 3 July 2025 with NOA Group Trading Proprietary Limited. The PPA is for
the supply of wheeled power for 10 years, with the option to extend it for another five years. The underlying solar
plant remains under construction with various supply agreements remaining outstanding for the plant to become
operational. The accounting treatment of recognising the PPA as an executory contract is unchanged from the
prior reporting period.
Contingent liabilities
The Group identified no material contingent liabilities in the current or previous reporting period.

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

**Note 19 — Reconciliation of PBT to cash generated by operations `[D006.10, physical PDF p. 35]`**
- **Key observation**: The reconciliation starts at PBT 534,368 and adds back 143,482 of adjustments, but the listed adjustment lines sum to **more than 143,482**. Let me check: 57,272 − 4,706 + 17,318 + 0 − 28,019 + 781 + 14,609 + 0 + 163 − 15,812 − 109 − 3,029 − 1,142 + 61,980 + 345 = **99,651** (FY26). This does **not** equal 143,482. There is a **~43,831 unexplained gap** in the "Adjusted for" subtotal. This is a material arithmetic tension — flag as a verification question. (Possibly the subtotal includes items not itemised, or the 57,272 SBP is presented gross of the 18,200 settlement, or there is a missing line.)
- **FY25 check**: 13,358 − 1,856 + 21,073 − 28,019 + 53 + 5,106 + 1,925 − 481 − 15,812 + 0 − 2,616 + 0 + 34,394 + 2,954 = **30,079** — this **does** reconcile exactly to the FY25 subtotal. So the FY26 subtotal of 143,482 is the anomaly. This strongly suggests a missing or misstated FY26 line item (~43,831). **High-priority verification question.**
- **Cash-settled SBP**: 57,272 expense vs 18,200 settlement — the 39,072 accrual build is a non-cash add-back; FY27 will see cash settlement of the 43,969 current portion (Q100). This is a **future cash outflow** to model.
- **Finance costs 17,318 vs D006.8 gross 12,666** — discrepancy of 4,652 (see Q85 above). Must reconcile.
- **Royalty costs 14,609** added back — this is a non-cash accrual? Or a reclassification? Royalties are normally cash costs; adding them back here suggests they are presented separately below "net cash from operating activities before dividend, tax, royalties and net finance costs." This means the **712,119** headline is **before royalties** — a key forecast input: FY27 royalties will be a cash outflow below this line.
- **Advance consideration received 8,030** — matches contract liability; FY27 delivery timing matters (Q46).
- **Rehabilitation costs incurred only 247** — confirms negligible near-term rehab cash spend (Q88 partial).

**Note 20.1 — Categories of financial instruments `[D006.10, physical PDF p. 35]`**
- **Borrowings 51,535 vs facilities 220,550** (from D006.8) — the carrying value is net of unamortised costs and excludes undrawn facilities. The US$78.9m "debt facilities" figure remains unreconciled (Q31).
- **Short-term investments 137,867 at FVTPL** — this is a large liquid balance; its treatment in net debt is critical (Q81/Q89). If included, net debt is materially lower.
- **Investment 12,705 (CuFe)** — Level 1, mark-to-market; FY27 FV movements will hit P&L.
- **Rehab fund 34,617** — Level 2; FV gain 3,029 in FY26 (Note 19). This is a non-cash gain added back; FY27 assumption needed.
- **Trade and other payables 126,369** vs 64,837 — near-doubling; consistent with the 50,354 working capital inflow. FY27 unwind risk (Q79).

**Note 20.2 — Fair value hierarchy `[D006.10, physical PDF p. 36]`**
- Footnotes 1–3 fully defined: CuFe Level 1 (ASX-traded); rehab fund Level 2 (insurance product, independent managers); STI Level 2 (NAV per unit from observable inputs). No missing footnote text. No `[^2]` gap here.
- **Implication**: STI 137,867 is Level 2, not Level 1 — slightly less liquid than cash; relevant for net debt/liquidity treatment.

**Note 21 — Commitments, contingent liabilities and guarantees `[D006.10, physical PDF p. 36]`**
- **Outstanding open orders 94,580** vs 36,500 — a **2.6x increase**, signalling a major capex/expansion ramp (likely Tennant, MTR, solar). This is a **forward capex indicator** — must tie to FY27 capex guidance (Q19/Q70/Q71).
- **Board-approved commitments not yet contracted 309,076** vs 106,300 — a **~3x increase**; this is a large pipeline of approved but uncontracted spend. Critical for FY27–FY28 capex forecast. Must identify which projects (Soweto Cluster? Tennant expansion? Solar?).
- **IFRS 16 lease commitments 3,214** (12 months) — modest.
- **Financial liability commitment 418** (12 months).
- **Guarantees — Eskom 5,134** (vs 3,761) — power supply guarantee.
- **DMPR guarantees 39,566** (vs 37,628) — **not** the 40,575 previously referenced (Q88); this is the environmental guarantee. Must confirm it matches the rehabilitation provision balance in D006.5/D006.6.
- **PPA footnote**: Solar plant **still under construction**; supply agreements outstanding; executory contract treatment unchanged. This confirms **no solar generation in FY26** and a **FY27 electricity cost step-up risk** once the PPA starts (Q40/Q72/Q96). The 10-year term + 5-year option is a long-dated fixed-cost commitment not quantified here — flag for D008.
- **Contingent liabilities**: "No material contingent liabilities" — clean, but note the guarantees above are disclosed separately.

22. RELATED PARTY TRANSACTIONS
The related party transactions are summarised as follows:
• Intra-Group interest and management fees – refer to segment analysis note 3
• Intra-Group loans have no specific repayment terms, are repayable on demand and bear interest in relation 
to the treasury function provided by Funding Company
• Intra-Group PAR Gold reciprocal dividend – refer to the summarised consolidated statement of changes 
in equity
• Intra-Group electricity charge between Evander Solar Solutions Proprietary Limited and Evander Mines for 
the electricity produced by the solar plant and utilised by Elikhulu – refer to segment analysis note 3.
No further material related party transactions occurred, either with third parties or with Group entities, during 
the current or previous reporting period.
23. LITIGATION AND CLAIMS
Evander Mines and MPC
Evander Mines terminated the contract mining agreement (CMA) with its 8 Shaft contractor during the 2024 
reporting period due to disputes over specific clauses in the CMA. Evander Mines referred this matter to 
arbitration and the proceedings are still ongoing. The likelihood of any outflow of economic benefits is remote.
	 Department of Forestry, Fisheries and the Environment – alleged offences in the 
Barberton Nature Reserve
On 22 May 2025, the South African state served a summons on Barberton Mines and its environmental health 
and safety manager for alleged contraventions of the National Environmental Management: Protected Areas 
Act, 57 of 2003, and related regulations. The charges relate to (i) conducting commercial prospecting in a 
nature reserve and (ii) the unauthorised widening and upgrading of a road within a nature reserve. Barberton 
Mines denies the merits of the charges and submitted formal written representations to the National Prosecuting 
Authority (NPA) on 17 September 2025, requesting the NPA to, inter alia, withdraw the charges. The NPA is still 
considering the representation and has been directed by the Court to provide Barberton Mines with a response 
on the representation by 29 September 2026. The likelihood of any outflow of economic benefits is remote.
Barberton Mines land claim
Barberton Mines is aware of a land claim, lodged by individuals purporting to be part of communities surrounding 
Barberton Mines’ Sheba Mine, pertaining to two portions of land, over which Barberton Mines holds two 
converted mining rights. Barberton Mines is formally opposing the land claim and has reasonable prospects to 
successfully challenge the claim. The Group’s legal counsel has advised that, irrespective of the merits of the 
claim, there should be no impact on the Company’s ability to exercise its mining right and continue operations.
24. EVENTS AFTER THE REPORTING PERIOD
As announced on the SENS, on 1 July 2026, the Company issued 102,641,421 new Pan African shares (in the 
form of ASX-listed Pan African CDIs) to eligible Emmerson shareholders in settlement for the acquisition of 100% 
of the issued shares in Emmerson.
There were no other events that could have a material impact on the financial results of the Group after 
30 June 2026 up to the date on which the Group financial statements for the reporting period ended 
30 June 2026 were authorised for issue

## SECTION 15: [D008.1] Alternative performance measures — FINANCIAL APMs
================================================================================

### [FORENSIC EXTRACTION: CAPTURED TEXT & FINANCIAL TABLES]
--- Section D008.1 | original physical page 38 | text selection | Commentary ---
INTRODUCTION
When assessing and discussing Pan African’s reported financial performance, financial position and cash flows, 
management makes reference to alternative performance measures (APMs) of historical or future financial performance, 
financial position or cash flows that are not defined or specified under IFRS Accounting Standards.
The APMs include financial APMs, non-financial APMs and ratios, as described below.
• Financial APMs: These financial measures are usually derived from the annual financial statements which have been 
prepared in accordance with IFRS Accounting Standards. Certain financial measures cannot be directly derived from 
the annual financial statements as they contain additional information such as financial information from earlier periods 
or profit estimates or projections. The accounting policies applied when calculating APMs are, where relevant and 
unless otherwise stated, the same as those disclosed in the consolidated financial statements for the year ended 
30 June 2026.
• Non-financial APMs: These measures incorporate certain non-financial information that management believes is useful 
when assessing the performance of the Group.
• Ratios: Ratios may be calculated using any of the APMs referred to above, IFRS Accounting Standards measures or 
a combination of APMs and IFRS Accounting Standards measures. APMs are not uniformly defined by all companies 
and may not be comparable with APM disclosures made by other companies, and they exclude:
– measures defined or specified by an applicable reporting framework such as revenue, profit or loss or earnings 
per share
– physical or non-financial measures such as number of employees, number of subscribers, revenue per unit 
measure (when the revenue figures are extracted directly from the annual financial statements) or social and 
environmental measures such as gas emissions, breakdown of workforce by contract or geographical location
– information on major shareholdings, acquisition or disposal of own shares and total number of voting rights
– information to explain the compliance with the terms of an agreement or legislative requirements such as lending 
covenants or the basis of calculating director or executive remuneration.
APMs should be considered in addition to, and not as a substitute for or as superior to, measures of financial 
performance, financial position or cash flows reported in accordance with IFRS Accounting Standards.
PURPOSE OF APMs
The Group uses APMs to improve the comparability of information between reporting periods and reporting segments by 
adjusting for uncontrollable or once-off factors which impact IFRS Accounting Standards measurements and disclosures 
to aid the user of the integrated annual report in understanding the activity taking place across the Group’s portfolio. 
The directors are responsible for preparing the APMs.
Their use is driven by characteristics particularly visible in the mining sector.
• Earnings volatility: The sector is characterised by significant volatility in earnings driven by movements in 
macroeconomic factors, primarily commodity prices and foreign exchange rates. This volatility is outside the 
control of management and can mask underlying changes in performance. As such, when comparing year-on-year 
performance, management excludes certain non-recurring items to aid comparability and then quantifies and isolates 
uncontrollable factors to improve understanding of the controllable portion of variances.
• Nature of investment: Investments in the sector are typically capital-intensive and occur over several years requiring 
significant funding before generating cash. These investments are often made through debt and equity providers, and 
the nature of the Group’s ownership interest affects how the financial results of these operations are reflected in the 
Group’s results, for example, whether full consolidation (subsidiaries), consolidation of the Group’s attributable assets 
and liabilities (joint operations) or equity-accounted (associates and joint ventures).
• Portfolio complexity: At year-end, the Group’s operating portfolio remains largely in commodities, mainly gold, which 
accounts for 99.7% of the Group’s revenue. The cost, value of and return from each saleable unit (such as tonne or 
ounce) therefore do not differ materially between each operating business. This makes understanding both the overall 
portfolio performance and the relative performance of each mining operation on a like-for-like basis less challenging.
Consequently, APMs are used by the board and management for planning and reporting. A subset is also used by 
management in setting director and management remuneration. The measures are also used in discussions with the 
investment analyst community and credit rating agencies.

### [SECTION READING RESPONSE / DETERMINATION]
### 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

- **Q2 & Q20 (AISC reconciliation by operation and group; confirm vs guidance US$1,820–1,870/oz; reconcile Elikhulu/MTR/BTRP to group AISC)**
  - **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D009)`
  - **Answer & evidence**: D008.1 provides the group AISC reconciliation at the "lower-cost vs higher-cost operations" level only, not by individual mine. FY26 group AISC = US$508,405k ÷ 272,373oz = **US$1,867/oz** [D008.1, p. 39]. This sits at the **top of the US$1,820–1,870/oz guidance range** (Q2 confirmed on the group number). Split: lower-cost operations US$420,686k ÷ 247,219oz = US$1,702/oz; higher-cost operations US$87,718k ÷ 25,154oz = US$3,487/oz. FY25 comparative: US$315,068k ÷ 196,927oz = US$1,600/oz (lower-cost US$1,434/oz; higher-cost US$2,598/oz). **The by-mine reconciliation (Elikhulu US$1,231/oz, MTR US$1,389/oz, BTRP US$1,564/oz) is NOT in this section** — it must be checked in D009 (Group production summary). Note the higher-cost bucket AISC deteriorated sharply YoY (US$2,598→US$3,487/oz) on lower ounces (28,146→25,154oz) — this is the "~US$3,500/oz tail" referenced in Q1 and needs mine-level attribution in D009.

- **Q34 (remainder) (Confirm APM reconciliation; AISC/APM definitions tie to IFRS)**
  - **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D008.2+)`
  - **Answer & evidence**: D008.1 confirms AISC is defined per the **World Gold Council** and is reconciled to "Cost of production" per IFRS, with adjustments for "other related costs as defined by the World Gold Council, including royalty costs, community costs, sustaining and development capital (excluding non-gold operations)" [D008.1, p. 38]. This confirms royalties ARE inside AISC (relevant to Q110). The actual line-by-line AISC reconciliation table (the "page 72 APM reconciliation" referenced in Q34) is **not in this capture** — still open.

- **Q36 (Confirm Adjusted EBITDA definition; restate FY25)**
  - **Status**: `Resolved (definition confirmed)`
  - **Answer & evidence**: Adjusted EBITDA = Profit after tax + income tax + depreciation & amortisation + net finance costs + impairment loss/reversals + bargain purchase gain + unrealised FV gains/losses on financial derivatives in the normal course of business [D008.1, p. 38]. Note: **SBP expense is NOT listed as an add-back** — so the US$57,272k SBP charge (Q101) sits INSIDE Adjusted EBITDA unless separately adjusted. The FY25 restatement arithmetic (249,724 + 2,954 = 252,678) cannot be re-verified here as the EBITDA reconciliation table is not in this capture.

AISC is defined off "Cost of production" and does not reference FV gains, so the STI gain is **not** in AISC. ### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

No footnote markers (`[^1]`, `*`) appear in the supplied D008.1 evidence. However, several embedded qualifications in the APM definitions carry material forecast implications:

- **AISC definition — "excluding non-gold operations"** [D008.1, p. 38]: The AISC adjustment explicitly excludes sustaining/development capital for non-gold operations. Since gold is 99.7% of revenue [D008.1, p. 38], this is immaterial for FY26 but must be checked in D009/D006.2 for any non-gold (e.g., chrome, PGM by-product) capital that is excluded from AISC but still consumes cash — a potential hidden cash drag not captured in AISC.

- **AISC definition — "value of by-product revenue is deducted from operating costs"** [D008.1, p. 39]: Confirms AISC is a **net** cost after by-product credits. For forecasting, any change in by-product (e.g., uranium, silver, chrome) prices/volumes will move AISC inversely. Need to check D009/D006.2 for by-product revenue quantum to model this sensitivity.

- **Adjusted EBITDA — SBP NOT an add-back** [D008.1, p. 38]: As flagged in Q101, the US$57,272k SBP charge sits inside Adjusted EBITDA. This is a **material** qualification: FY27 Adjusted EBITDA will be depressed by the FY27 SBP charge (which depends on share price and vesting). Forecast must model SBP as a real cost, not strip it out.

- **Adjusted EBITDA — "unrealised fair value gains or losses on financial derivatives undertaken in the normal course of business"** [D008.1, p. 38]: The qualifier "in the normal course of business" is important — it means hedging derivatives are added back, but speculative/non-normal-course derivatives are not. Need to confirm in D008.2+ whether the STI (137,867) and gold trading facilities (58,931) are classified as "normal course" derivatives for this purpose.

- **Free cash flow — "Less capital expenditure funded through permitted indebtedness"** [D008.1, p. 39]: This is a non-obvious adjustment. FCF is reduced by capex funded through permitted indebtedness — meaning debt-funded capex is treated as a cash outflow in FCF even though it may not hit the cash flow statement as capex. For FY27 forecasting, need to identify which capex (Tennant crusher/mill/filter, MTR expansion, solar) is "permitted indebtedness"-funded and therefore deducted in FCF but not in reported capex. This could create a gap between reported capex and FCF-deducted capex.

- **Free cash flow — "Adjusted for non-cash flow items as determined in accordance with IAS 7"** [D008.1, p. 39]: This is a catch-all that could include SBP, rehabilitation provisions, etc. Need to check the FCF reconciliation table (not in this capture) to see exactly which non-cash items are adjusted — particularly whether SBP is added back in FCF (even though it's not in Adjusted EBITDA).

- **Levered free cash flow — includes "Proceeds from borrowings" and "Repayment of borrowings"** [D008.1, p. 39]: This means LFCF is a **post-financing** metric. For FY27, the RCF maturity (30 June 2027) and any refinancing will flow through LFCF. Need to check D008.2+ for the LFCF reconciliation and whether the RCF repayment is modelled.

- **Net debt — "cash and related hedges"** [D008.1, p. 39]: The phrase "related hedges" is ambiguous — does it mean hedges related to cash (e.g., FX forwards on cash balances) or hedges related to debt (e.g., interest rate swaps)? This affects whether the STI (137,867) is netted against debt. Need to check the net debt reconciliation table.

- **Net debt — "Financial liabilities" adjustment** [D008.1, p. 39]: This is a broad catch-all. Could include the cash-settled SBP current portion (43,969), the gold loan facility (25,557), and gold trading facilities (58,931). Need to check the reconciliation table to confirm which financial liabilities are added to net debt.

- **Headline earnings — per SAICA Circular 1/2023** [D008.1, p. 39]: Confirms headline earnings is calculated per SAICA Circular 1/2023, with adjustments for (profit)/loss on disposal of PPE, impairment/reversals, bargain purchase gain, and tax effect. This is the JSE-listed headline EPS metric — relevant for dividend cover and valuation, but not directly for AISC/EBITDA forecasting.

- **"Portfolio complexity" — gold 99.7% of revenue** [D008.1, p. 38]: Confirms the group is essentially a pure gold play. For forecasting, this means the gold price deck (Q30) is the dominant revenue driver, and by-product credits are immaterial (<0.3% of revenue).

- **"Earnings volatility" — management excludes non-recurring items and quantifies uncontrollable factors** [D008.1, p. 38]: This is a forward-looking statement of intent — management will continue to present APMs that strip out price/FX volatility. For forecasting, this means reported APMs may diverge from IFRS numbers in FY27 if there are further "non-recurring" items (e.g., Emmerson integration costs, solar disposal gain).

| AISC adjustments include | Royalty costs, community costs, sustaining & development capital (excl. non-gold ops); by-product revenue deducted | — | [D008.1, p. 38–39] |
| Adjusted EBITDA add-backs | Income tax, D&A, net finance costs, impairment/reversals, bargain purchase gain, unrealised FV gains/losses on normal-course derivatives | — | [D008.1, p. 38] |
| SBP expense in Adjusted EBITDA? | **No add-back — remains inside** | — | [D008.1, p. 38] |
| Net debt definition | Borrowings from financial institutions + IFRS 16 leases − cash & related hedges; adj. IFRS 9, restricted cash, financial liabilities | — | [D008.1, p. 39] |
| FCF definition | PAT + tax + D&A + net finance costs + impairment + unrealised FV − WC changes − IAS 7 non-cash − capex funded via permitted indebtedness − tax paid | — | [D008.1, p. 39] |
| LFCF definition | PAT + tax + D&A + net finance costs + impairment + unrealised FV − finance costs paid − tax paid − NWC − capex + borrowings proceeds − repayments | — | [D008.1, p. 39] |
| Headline earnings basis | SAICA Circular 1/2023; adj. (profit)/loss on PPE disposal, impairment/reversals, bargain purchase gain, tax effect | — | [D008.1, p. 39] |

If D008.2 is not available, **`D009` (Group production summary, pp. 48–50)** is the next best — it will resolve **Q2 (remainder), Q20 (remainder), Q124, Q125** and the by-mine AISC reconciliation.

--- Section D008.2 | original physical page 40 | area selection | Commentary ---
                                                           
   ALL-IN COSTS                                            
   This includes additional costs which relate to the growth of the Group. AIC starts with AISC and adds additional costs
   which relate to the growth of the Group, including non-sustaining capital expenditure not associated with current
   operations and costs such as voluntary severance pay.   
   AISC and AIC are reported on the basis of a ZAR per kilogramme of gold1 and US$ per ounce of gold. The US$
   equivalent is converted at the average exchange rate applicable for the current reporting period as disclosed in the
   Group’s operational production table on pages 92 to 97. A kilogramme of gold is converted to an ounce of gold at
   a ratio of 1:32.1509.                                   
   The following tables set out a reconciliation of Pan African’s cost of production as calculated in accordance with
   IFRS Accounting Standards to AISC and AIC for the financial years ended 30 June 2026 and 30 June 2025.
   The equivalent of the ZAR per kilogramme1 and US$ per ounce basis is disclosed in the Group’s operational
   production table on pages 92 to 97.                     
   1 A$ per kilogramme of gold.

### [SECTION READING RESPONSE / DETERMINATION]
### 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

**Q118: Locate full AISC reconciliation table (cost of production → AISC); confirm line items (royalties, community, sustaining/development capex, by-product credits); tie US$508,405k total.**
- **Status**: `Resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: The full reconciliation is on p. 40. Group gold cost of production US$435.7m + royalties 20.8 + community 1.9 − by-product credits 4.1 + corporate G&A 34.9 − reclamation accretion 0.6 + sustaining development 2.4 + sustaining maintenance 17.5 = **US$508.4m AISC** (table shows 508.4; the 508,405k figure ties to rounding). All requested line items are present. [D008.2, p. 40]

**Q124: By-mine AISC table; reconcile each mine to group US$508,405k; confirm lower-cost vs higher-cost bucket membership.**
- **Status**: `Resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: By-mine AISC (US$m): Barberton 181.4, Evander 90.2, BTRP 20.1, Elikhulu 70.3, MTR 73.8, Tennant 72.6 = **508.4**. Lower-cost bucket (AISC < group US$1,867/oz): Elikhulu US$1,231/oz, MTR US$1,389/oz, BTRP US$1,564/oz, Evander US$1,914/oz (marginally above group). Higher-cost: Barberton US$2,589/oz, Tennant US$2,261/oz. [D008.2, pp. 40, 42]

**Q125: Identify higher-cost bucket mines; explain AISC rise US$2,598→US$3,487/oz; FY27 outlook.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D009)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Group AISC rose from US$1,600/oz (FY25) to US$1,867/oz (FY26) — a US$267/oz increase, not the US$2,598→US$3,487 cited in the stored question (that figure appears to be a different metric or mis-transcription; flag for D009). Drivers visible here: (i) ZAR strengthened 18.17→16.90 (raises US$ costs); (ii) Barberton AISC US$2,065→US$2,589/oz on higher sustaining capex (6.4→9.8 maintenance) and royalties (4.7→9.0); (iii) Tennant AISC US$1,637→US$2,261/oz on first full year of operation and A$ strength (0.65→0.68). FY27 outlook not in this section — carry to D009. [D008.2, pp. 40–43]

**Q126: Confirm non-gold capital excluded from AISC; quantify if material.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D006.5/D009)`
- **Answer & evidence**: AIC definition (p. 40) explicitly adds "non-sustaining capital expenditure not associated with current operations" and "voluntary severance pay" on top of AISC. FY26 AIC US$703.4m vs AISC US$508.4m — expansion capex US$195.0m is the bridge. No explicit non-gold capital line is shown; the FY25 table shows a voluntary severance line (1.4) that is absent in FY26. Whether any non-gold (e.g., solar, exploration) capex sits inside the 195.0 expansion figure needs checking against Note 7/D009. [D008.2, pp. 40–41]

**Q127: Quantify by-product revenue deducted from AISC; confirm FY27 assumption.**
- **Status**: `Resolved (FY26 quantum); FY27 assumption open`
- **Answer & evidence**: FY26 by-product credits total **US$4.1m** (Barberton 0.2, Evander 1.0, MTR 1.2, Tennant 1.7). FY25 was US$1.0m. FY27 assumption not stated here — carry forward. [D008.2, pp. 40–41]

**Q63 (remainder): Confirm AISC uses production D&A US$60,288k (not total PPE depreciation US$61,733k); identify US$2,179k "other D&A".**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved — contradiction flagged`
- **Answer & evidence**: The AISC reconciliation on p. 40 contains **no D&A line at all**. Cost of production (435.7) flows straight to cash cost with no depreciation add-back shown, and the bridge to AISC uses only royalties, community, by-product credits, corporate G&A, reclamation, and sustaining capex. This is inconsistent with the stored assumption that AISC embeds production D&A of US$60,288k. Either (a) D&A is embedded inside the 435.7 cost-of-production figure and not separately disclosed, or (b) the reconciliation omits it. Must be verified against Note 3/Note 7 and the operational production table (pp. 92–97). [D008.2, p. 40]

**Q110 (resolved definitional — royalties ARE in AISC; model as AISC component).**
- **Status**: `Confirmed`
- **Answer & evidence**: Royalties are an explicit add-back line: FY26 US$20.8m (Barberton 9.0, Evander 0.9, BTRP 0.1, Tennant 10.8). [D008.2, p. 40]

**Q101 (resolved definitional — SBP NOT excluded from Adjusted EBITDA/AISC; model as real cost).**
- **Status**: `Confirmed by absence`
- **Answer & evidence**: No SBP add-back or exclusion line appears anywhere in the AISC/AIC reconciliation. SBP therefore sits inside cost of production / corporate G&A and is a real cost. [D008.2, pp. 40–41]

**Q2 (remainder): By-mine AISC reconciliation (Elikhulu US$1,231/oz, MTR US$1,389/oz, BTRP US$1,564/oz) to group US$508,405k.**
- **Status**: `Resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: Confirmed — Elikhulu US$1,231/oz (US$70.3m / 57,092oz), MTR US$1,389/oz (US$73.8m / 53,133oz), BTRP US$1,564/oz (US$20.1m / 12,826oz). Sum of all six operations = US$508.4m. [D008.2, pp. 40, 42]

**Q6: Confirm Elikhulu 56,475oz @ US$1,231/oz and MTR 51,927oz @ US$1,389/oz full-year; MTR expansion run-rate FY27.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved — volume discrepancy flagged`
- **Answer & evidence**: AISC rates confirmed (Elikhulu US$1,231/oz, MTR US$1,389/oz). However, **gold sold** volumes here are Elikhulu **57,092oz** and MTR **53,133oz** — not the 56,475oz / 51,927oz in the stored question. The difference (617oz Elikhulu, 1,206oz MTR) may be production vs. sold, or a Highlights-vs-APM basis difference. Flag for D009 reconciliation. MTR expansion run-rate not in this section. [D008.2, p. 42]

**Q20 (remainder): Reconcile Elikhulu/MTR/BTRP AISC to group AISC.**

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

**Footnote [^1] on p. 40 (FY26 table):** *"This total may not reflect the sum of the line items due to rounding."*
- **What it changes**: The group AISC of 508.4 and AIC of 703.4 are subject to rounding; the underlying sum of by-mine figures (181.4+90.2+20.1+70.3+73.8+72.6 = 508.4) ties, but individual line-item sums within columns may differ by ±0.1. For forecasting, use the by-mine figures as the primary build and treat the group total as a check, not a plug. [D008.2, p. 40]

**Footnote [^1] on p. 41 (FY25 table):** *"The above table was disclosed in ZAR million in the 2024 integrated annual report."*
- **What it changes**: The FY25 comparative has been **re-presented from ZAR to US$** using the FY25 average rate of 18.17. This means FY25 US$ figures are not directly comparable to the originally published ZAR figures and may differ from any prior-year US$ disclosure. For YoY analysis, the FX translation itself (18.17→16.90) is a material driver of the US$ AISC increase — roughly 7% of the US$1,600→1,867/oz move is pure FX. Must verify against the 2024 IAR ZAR figures if a clean constant-currency comparison is needed. [D008.2, p. 41]

**Footnote [^1] on p. 42 (FY26 unit table):** *"This total may not reflect the sum of the line items due to rounding."*
- **What it changes**: Same rounding caveat applies to the per-unit (US$/oz, ZAR/kg, A$/kg) table. Note the **A$/kg column for Tennant** uses a separate A$ cost base (A$81.0m cost of production, A$106.8m AISC, A$205.1m AIC) translated at 0.68 — this is the only operation with a non-ZAR functional currency and must be modelled separately. [D008.2, p. 42]

**Footnote [^1] on p. 43 (FY25 unit table):** *"This total may not reflect the sum of the line items due to rounding."*
- **What it changes**: Same caveat. Critically, the FY25 Tennant AIC of **US$33,693/oz** (and A$1,666,455/kg) is an artefact of a near-zero production base (37kg / 1,179oz) against A$61.1m AIC — this is **not a meaningful run-rate** and must be excluded from any trend analysis. FY26 Tennant AIC of US$4,339/oz is the first meaningful full-year figure. [D008.2, p. 43]

**Embedded qualification on p. 40 (AIC definition):** *"AIC starts with AISC and adds additional costs which relate to the growth of the Group, including non-sustaining capital expenditure not associated with current operations and costs such as voluntary severance pay."*
- **What it changes**: The AISC→AIC bridge (508.4→703.4, +195.0) is **entirely expansion capex** in FY26 — no voluntary severance line appears (unlike FY25's 1.4). This means FY26 AIC is a "growth-heavy" number and the 195.0 expansion capex is the key FY27 forecast lever. If expansion capex steps down in FY27 (e.g., Tennant build completion), AIC should compress toward AISC. [D008.2, pp. 40–41]

**Embedded qualification on p. 40 (FX methodology):** *"The US$ equivalent is converted at the average exchange rate applicable for the current reporting period as disclosed in the Group's operational production table on pages 92 to 97."*
- **What it changes**: The US$ AISC is a **translation of ZAR/A$ costs at the period average**, not a transaction-level rate. FY27 US$ AISC will therefore move with the ZAR/A$ average even if local-currency costs are flat. The 16.90 (FY26) vs 18.17 (FY25) move alone accounts for a meaningful chunk of the US$ AISC increase. Pages 92–97 are outside this capture — flag for D009. [D008.2, p. 40]

**Arithmetic tension flagged:** The p. 40 FY26 table shows "Gold cost of production" of 435.7 and "Cash cost" of 435.7 — identical. There is **no depreciation add-back** between cost of production and cash cost, and no D&A line in the AISC bridge. This is unusual for a gold miner's AISC reconciliation (most show "adjusted for D&A" or similar). Either D&A is embedded in the 435.7 and not separately disclosed, or the reconciliation is on a cash-cost basis throughout. This directly contradicts stored Q63's assumption that AISC uses production D&A of US$60,288k. **Must be resolved against Note 3/Note 7 and the operational production table before modelling AISC.** [D008.2, p. 40]

**Arithmetic tension flagged:** FY26 "Corporate general and administrative costs" column layout is garbled in the capture (13.7 | 2.4 | 16.1 | – 6.9 | 6.4 | 5.5 | 18.8 | 13.7 | 9.3 | 6.4 | 5.5 | 34.9). The group total of 34.9 does not obviously sum from the visible sub-columns (16.1 + 6.9 + 6.4 + 5.5 = 34.9 — so the "– 6.9" is likely Elikhulu 6.9 and the 18.8 is a subtotal). The by-mine allocation of corporate G&A (Barberton 13.7, Evander 2.4, Elikhulu 6.9, MTR 6.4, Tennant 5.5) sums to 34.9. Flag for re-capture if precise by-mine G&A is needed. [D008.2, p. 40]

**Arithmetic tension flagged:** FY26 "Community cost related to gold operations" row shows Barberton 1.1, Evander –, BTRP –, Elikhulu –, MTR 0.8, Tennant –, group 1.9. But 1.1 + 0.8 = 1.9, so the group total ties; however the column alignment in the capture places 0.8 under MTR and the group total under a different column. Minor — flag for re-capture if by-mine community cost is needed. [D008.2, p. 40]

### 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

**Q120 (net debt reconciliation — treatment of gold loan, gold trading, STI, cash-settled SBP, restricted cash, IFRS 16 leases)**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D006.8 / D008.1)`
- **Answer & evidence**: The D008.3 net (cash)/debt reconciliation `[D008.3, physical PDF p. 44]` shows: Cash and cash equivalents (108.3); Short-term investments (137.9); Borrowings 51.5; Financial instrument liability – (FY25: 1.8); Lease liability 7.8 (FY25: 3.9); Financial liability 1.2 (FY25: 3.3); Facility arrangement fees – (FY25: 0.9); **Net (cash)/debt (185.7)** vs FY25 150.5. Critically, **no separate line for gold loan (25,557) or gold trading (58,931) appears** — these must therefore be embedded inside "Borrowings 51.5" or excluded entirely. This is a material tension: 25.557 + 58.931 = 84.488, which exceeds total borrowings of 51.5, so at least one of the two must be excluded from net debt (or netted elsewhere). **Cash-settled SBP (43,969) is NOT visible as a line** — it is not in net debt per this table. **Restricted cash (2,612) is not separately shown** — it is presumably inside the 108.3 cash figure (i.e., not deducted). STI (137.9) **is** netted in net debt. Remainder (which of gold loan / gold trading sits in Borrowings 51.5, and where SBP 43,969 sits) stays open for D006.8/D008.1.

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

- **No footnote markers (`[^1]`, `*`) are present in the supplied D008.3 evidence** `[D008.3, p. 44]`. There is therefore no buried footnote commentary to unpack on this page. This is itself notable: the two most consequential definitional caveats are in **body text**, not footnotes:
  1. **Net debt definition** `[D008.3, p. 44]`: "total borrowings from financial institutions (**before IFRS 9 accounting adjustments**) less cash and cash equivalents (**including derivatives** entered into in connection with protection against, or benefit from, fluctuations in exchange rates or commodity prices)." — This is a **non-IFRS definition**: it excludes IFRS 9 adjustments (so the balance-sheet borrowings figure will differ from the 51.5 here) and it **nets commodity/FX hedges against cash**. Implication: the 108.3 "cash" is a net-of-hedge figure, and the true gross cash may be higher. Must verify against the statement of financial position (D005) and Note 8/9 (D006.6).
  2. **Net senior debt definition** `[D008.3, p. 44]`: "secured, interest-bearing debt provided by financial institutions, net of available cash." — Excludes leases (7.8) and financial liability (1.2), and excludes STI? No — STI (137.9) **is** netted in senior debt too (shown as negative), giving net senior debt **(194.7)** vs net debt (185.7). The 9.0 difference = lease 7.8 + financial liability 1.2. This confirms leases and the 1.2 financial liability are **excluded** from senior debt but **included** in net debt.
- **Table structural anomaly** `[D008.3, p. 44]`: The sustaining/expansion capital table has **misaligned columns** in the capture (e.g., "Mining operations 12.2 | 8.3 | 25.3 16.6" — the FY25/FY26 pairing is ambiguous). The **Group total row is internally consistent**: sustaining 21.8 (FY26) / 11.7 (FY25); expansion 202.2 (FY26) / 156.2 (FY25); total 224.0 (FY26) / 167.9 (FY25). Check: 21.8 + 202.2 = 224.0 ✓; 11.7 + 156.2 = 167.9 ✓. But the **by-mine rows do not visibly sum to the group total** in the captured layout — e.g., expansion by mine: 27.5 + 69.2 + 29.9 + 68.4 + 7.2 = 202.2 ✓ (this does reconcile). Sustaining by mine: 12.6 + 1.7 + 2.7 + 2.9 + 0.1 + 1.8 = 21.8 ✓. So the totals reconcile; the column misalignment is a capture artefact, not a data error. **However**, the "Mining operations" sub-rows (Barberton 12.2/8.3; Evander –/–; etc.) appear to be **sub-components** of the mine totals, not additive — verify in D008.2/D006.2.
- **Key hidden implication**: The **195.0 vs 202.2** expansion capex gap (7.2 = solar) is the single most forecast-relevant item on this page — it tells us the AIC/AISC expansion add-back **excludes solar**, so FY27 AIC will not carry solar capex. Must confirm in D008.2.

### [FORENSIC EXTRACTION: CAPTURED TEXT & FINANCIAL TABLES]

Adjusted EBITDA is a measure of the Group’s operating performance and is calculated as net profit or loss for the 
Group before finance income and finance costs and tax, before any amount attributable to the amortisation of intangible 
assets and the depreciation of tangible assets and before any extraordinary items or the impairment of non-financial 
assets, bargain purchase gain and unrealised fair value loss on financial derivatives. A reconciliation of the Group’s 
adjusted EBITDA is provided in note 3 to the summarised financial statements.
A reconciliation of the adjusted EBITDA by operation has been provided below

NET ADJUSTED EBITDA
Net adjusted EBITDA starts with adjusted EBITDA adjusted for any entries made to unrealised fair value gains or 
losses on financial derivatives that are entered into in the normal course of business as part of the Group’s financial risk 
management process.
FREE CASH FLOW
Free cash flow starts with adjusted EBITDA and is adjusted for changes in net working capital, non-cash flow items as 
determined by IAS 7, capital expenditure less capital funded through permitted indebtedness and tax payments.
HEADLINE EARNINGS
Headline earnings, a JSE-defined performance measure (as defined by Circular 2023/1 issued by SAICA), are reconciled 
from profit/(loss) after tax in note 13 to the summarised financial statements.
RATIOS
Return on shareholder funds
This ratio measures returns to equity shareholders as a percentage of the capital invested in the Group. It is calculated as 
profit/(loss) after tax expressed as a percentage of the average total equity for the current and previous reporting periods.
Net debt-to-equity ratio
This ratio measures the degree to which the Group finances its operations through debt relative to equity and is calculated 
as net debt divided by total equity.
Net debt-to-net adjusted EBITDA ratio
This ratio measures the number of years it would take the Group to repay its net debt from net adjusted EBITDA, 
assuming both variables are held consistent, and is calculated as net debt divided by net adjusted EBITDA.
Interest cover ratio
This ratio measures the Group’s ability to pay interest on its outstanding senior debt from net adjusted EBITDA and is 
calculated as total net adjusted EBITDA divided by finance costs incurred on interest-bearing debt.
Debt service cover ratio
This ratio measures the cash flow available for debt service relative to the Group’s obligatory principal and interest debt 
obligations and is calculated as free cash flow available for debt service divided by principal and interest-debt obligations

--- Section D008.4 | original physical page 46 | area selection | Table ---
NET ASSET VALUE PER SHARE
This is calculated as total equity divided by the total number of shares in issue less treasury shares held by the Group.

- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D006.2 / D006.5)`
- **Answer & evidence**: The by-operation reconciliation is on `[D008.4, physical PDF p. 45]`. It starts from "Net income/(cost) before finance income and finance costs" and adds back "Mining depreciation and amortisation" and "(Profit)/loss on disposal of plant and equipment" to reach Adjusted EBITDA. **No share-based payment (SBP) line appears** — consistent with SBP NOT being added back. **No STI fair-value gain line appears** in the FY26 reconciliation (only an "Unrealised fair value loss on financial derivatives" line of 1.9 in FY25), so the STI FV gain add-back is not evidenced here and remains open. **No impairment line appears** in either year's reconciliation, so the impairment quantum is not evidenced here. The FY25 "Adjusted EBITDA" group total in this table is **245.8**, not the 252,678 (US$252.7m) referenced in Q119 — a discrepancy to resolve against the Note 3 reconciliation. `[D008.4, p. 45]`

**Q128: Confirm STI (137,867) and gold trading (58,931) classified as "normal course" derivatives for Adjusted EBITDA add-back.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D008.1 / D006.10)`
- **Answer & evidence**: The definition on `[D008.4, p. 45]` excludes only "unrealised fair value loss on financial derivatives" from Adjusted EBITDA; the "Net Adjusted EBITDA" definition on `[D008.4, p. 46]` adjusts for "unrealised fair value gains or losses on financial derivatives that are entered into in the normal course of business as part of the Group's financial risk management process." This confirms the *mechanism* for a normal-course derivative add-back but does **not** confirm the specific STI (137,867) or gold trading (58,931) amounts are so classified. Remains open.

**Q131: Confirm rehab fund FV gain treatment in Adjusted EBITDA.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D006.10)`
- **Answer & evidence**: The Adjusted EBITDA definition `[D008.4, p. 45]` excludes "unrealised fair value loss on financial derivatives" and "impairment of non-financial assets, bargain purchase gain" but makes **no mention of rehabilitation fund fair-value gains/losses**. The reconciliation table `[D008.4, p. 45]` shows no rehab-fund line. So there is no evidence the rehab fund FV gain is added back/excluded — it appears to remain inside "Net income before finance income and finance costs." Remains open for confirmation against Note 10/Note 19.

**Q121: Locate FCF reconciliation; identify "permitted indebtedness"-funded capex and IAS 7 non-cash adjustments; reconcile to cash flow statement.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (definition located; reconciliation not in this capture)`
- **Answer & evidence**: The FCF *definition* is on `[D008.4, p. 46]`: "starts with adjusted EBITDA and is adjusted for changes in net working capital, non-cash flow items as determined by IAS 7, capital expenditure less capital funded through permitted indebtedness and tax payments." The actual FCF reconciliation table is **not present** in this capture (only the Levered FCF table is shown). Remains open.

**Q122: Locate LFCF reconciliation; confirm RCF repayment (30 June 2027 maturity) modelled; tie to net debt movement.**
- **Status**: `Resolved (reconciliation located)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Levered FCF reconciliation on `[D008.4, p. 46]`: Adjusted EBITDA 609.4 − finance costs paid 15.0 − income tax paid 74.1 + net working capital movement 46.6 − capex 215.8 + proceeds from borrowings 0 − repayment of borrowings 149.3 = **LFCF 201.8** (FY25: 47.5). Note the LFCF starting Adjusted EBITDA is **609.4**, which differs from the by-operation table's 663.1 `[D008.4, p. 45]` — a reconciliation gap to resolve. RCF maturity/repayment modelling is not evidenced here; remains open for D006.8.

**Q123: Locate headline earnings reconciliation; confirm disposal/impairment/tax adjustments and EPS denominator.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (definition only)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Only the *definition* is on `[D008.4, p. 46]`: "Headline earnings, a JSE-defined performance measure (as defined by Circular 2023/1 issued by SAICA), are reconciled from profit/(loss) after tax in note 13." The actual reconciliation is **not in this capture**. EPS denominator: shares in issue 2,333.7m less treasury 306.4m = **2,027.3m** used for attributable cash flow per share `[D008.4, p. 46]`. Remains open for Note 13.

**Q132: Identify IAS 7 non-cash items adjusted in FCF; confirm SBP treatment in FCF.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (definition only)`
- **Answer & evidence**: FCF definition references "non-cash flow items as determined by IAS 7" `[D008.4, p. 46]` but the itemised reconciliation is not in this capture. SBP treatment in FCF not evidenced. Remains open.

**Q86 (remainder): Confirm RCF renewal/refinancing plans before 30 June 2027 maturity; FY27 liquidity headroom.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (indirect)`
- **Answer & evidence**: LFCF table shows FY26 "Repayment of borrowings 149.3" and "Proceeds from borrowings –" `[D008.4, p. 46]`, i.e., net debt repayment in FY26 with no new drawdowns. No RCF maturity/refinancing commentary in this capture. Remains open for D006.8.

**Q81 (remainder): Confirm STI FV gain US$1,142k excluded from Adjusted EBITDA/AISC.**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (mechanism only)`
- **Answer & evidence**: Net Adjusted EBITDA definition adjusts for normal-course derivative FV gains/losses `[D008.4, p. 46]`; the specific 1,142 STI gain is not shown in this capture. Remains open.

**Q36 (remainder): Confirm FY25 restated Adjusted EBITDA 252,678.**
- **Status**: `Contradicted / discrepancy found`
- **Answer & evidence**: The by-operation table shows FY25 group Adjusted EBITDA of **245.8** `[D008.4, p. 45]`, and the LFCF table shows FY25 Adjusted EBITDA of **226.6** `[D008.4, p. 46]`. Neither equals 252,678 (US$252.7m). Three different FY25 Adjusted EBITDA figures now exist — must be reconciled to Note 3. Remains open.

*(Stored questions belonging to other sections not evidenced here are carried forward in Section 4.)*

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

- **Footnote `[^1]` on p. 46 (LFCF yield):** "Amounts converted at the 30 June 2026 closing exchange rate of US$/ZAR:16.46 (FY25: US$/ZAR:17.75)." This is the FX rate used to convert the last traded price (125.09 US cents) and hence the 7.96% cash flow yield. **Implication:** the yield is FX-sensitive; a weaker ZAR inflates the US-cent share price and yield. Note this 16.46 close rate differs from the 16.90 average used elsewhere (Q4) — confirm which rate underpins FY27 dividend/FX assumptions. `[D008.4, p. 46]`

- **Footnote `[^1]` on p. 47 (dividend yield):** "Includes the maiden interim dividend of ZA 12.00 cents (0.55 pence) per share." **Implication:** FY26 DPS of 77.00 ZA cents is the *full-year* figure including the maiden interim; the FY26 dividend is therefore not a clean run-rate for FY27 unless the interim is annualised. Must confirm FY27 dividend policy and whether 77.00 is a sustainable base. `[D008.4, p. 47]`

- **Embedded qualification — Adjusted EBITDA definition (p. 45):** Excludes "impairment of non-financial assets, bargain purchase gain and unrealised fair value loss on financial derivatives." **Implication:** the FY25 bargain gain of (28.0) at Tennant is added back to reach FY25 Adjusted EBITDA of 2.4 for Tennant `[D008.4, p. 45]` — i.e., Tennant's FY25 underlying operating contribution was near-zero once the one-off bargain gain is stripped. This is a critical base-effect caveat for FY27 Tennant forecasting.

- **Embedded qualification — Net Adjusted EBITDA (p. 46):** Adjusts for normal-course derivative FV gains/losses. **Implication:** creates a second EBITDA layer (net adjusted) used for leverage/interest cover ratios; must confirm which EBITDA (adjusted 663.1 vs 609.4 vs net adjusted) feeds the net debt/EBITDA and interest cover ratios.

- **Arithmetic tension flagged:** By-operation Adjusted EBITDA group total **663.1** `[p. 45]` vs LFCF starting Adjusted EBITDA **609.4** `[p. 46]` — a **53.7** gap. Also FY25: 245.8 vs 226.6 = **19.2** gap. These must be reconciled (likely corporate costs / net adjusted derivative adjustments) before using either as the forecast base.

### [SECTION READING RESPONSE / DETERMINATION]
### 1. Answers to Stored Questions for This Section

**Q1: Confirm total group ounces, Sheba+Consort ounces and AISC; reconcile to ~US$3,500/oz tail. [D002, p.2–3]**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D002/D003)`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 confirms FY26 group gold produced = **272,310oz** and gold sold = **272,373oz** [D009, p.49]. Group US$ AISC = **US$1,867/oz** [D009, p.49]. The ~US$3,500/oz "tail" figure is **not** visible in D009 — no Sheba/Consort line appears in this production summary. Remainder (Sheba+Consort ounces and the US$3,500/oz tail) must be checked in D002/D003.

**Q5 (remainder): Confirm Tennant FY27 capex $; White Devil first blast date and grade ramp. [D002, p.3; D003, p.6]**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D002/D003)`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 gives FY26 Tennant capex = **US$71.3m** (A$? — table shows 71.3 in the US$ million capex row) [D009, p.50]. No FY27 capex guidance, White Devil blast date, or grade ramp is in D009. Remainder stays open.

**Q6 (remainder): Confirm Elikhulu/MTR full-year volumes — APM shows 57,092oz / 53,133oz sold vs stored 56,475oz / 51,927oz; reconcile production vs sold. [D008.2, p.42]**
- **Status**: `Resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 reconciles this exactly. **Elikhulu**: produced **56,475oz**, sold **57,092oz** [D009, p.49]. **MTR**: produced **51,927oz**, sold **53,133oz** [D009, p.49]. The APM figures (57,092 / 53,133) are **gold sold**; the stored 56,475 / 51,927 are **gold produced**. Difference is inventory drawdown (sold > produced at both operations in FY26).

**Q7 (remainder): Confirm Evander underground grade >11g/t FY26 sustainability into FY27. [D002, p.3]**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (remainder stays open for D002/D003)`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 confirms Evander **head grade excluding third-party buy-in feedstock = 11.2g/t** FY26 (vs 7.0g/t FY25) [D009, p.48]. However, **total head grade = 4.8g/t** and **overall recovered grade = 4.6g/t** because 202,555t of third-party buy-in feedstock at low grade dilutes the milled grade [D009, p.48]. Sustainability into FY27 not stated here — stays open.

**Q9: Reconcile White Devil ~3Mt @ 3.8g/t (~350Koz) to 50→100Koz/yr ramp over 5 years. [D002, p.3]**
- **Status**: `Unresolved — not addressed in D009`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 shows Tennant FY26 surface feedstock processed = **751,914t** at **1.4g/t** head grade, producing **32,124oz** [D009, p.48–49]. No White Devil-specific tonnage/grade or ramp schedule is disclosed in D009. Carry forward.

**Q10 (remainder): Confirm Royal Sheba first production timing; not double-counted with BTRP. [D002, p.3; D003, p.6]**
- **Status**: `Unresolved — not addressed in D009`
- **Answer & evidence**: No Royal Sheba line in D009. Carry forward.

**Q14: Check FY27 MTR production split; H1 FY27 below nameplate, Q2–Q4 step-up to ~60Koz annualised. [D003, p.6]**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (baseline only)`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 confirms FY26 MTR **produced 51,927oz / sold 53,133oz**, processed **12,178,487t** (11,566,768t tailings + 611,719t third-party buy-in) at **0.3g/t** head grade, **47% recovery** [D009, p.48–49]. FY27 split/step-up not in D009 — stays open.

**Q15: Check FY27 BTRP guidance; flotation benefit FY27 or FY28. [D003, p.6]**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (baseline only)`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 confirms FY26 BTRP **produced 12,932oz / sold 12,826oz**, processed **846,623t** (775,385t tailings + 71,238t third-party buy-in) at **1.4g/t** head grade, **33% recovery** [D009, p.48–49]. FY27 guidance/flotation timing not in D009 — stays open.

**Q17: Check Elikhulu FY27 guidance vs FY26 57,092oz sold; Winkelhaak feed contribution. [D003, p.6; D008.2, p.42]**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (baseline only)`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 confirms Elikhulu FY26 **produced 56,475oz / sold 57,092oz**, processed **13,807,025t** tailings at **0.3g/t**, **39% recovery** [D009, p.48–49]. FY27 guidance and Winkelhaak feed not in D009 — stays open.

**Q18: Check FY27 Tennant grade assumption and White Devil strip ratio/grade. [D003, p.6]**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (baseline only)`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 confirms Tennant FY26 **surface feedstock 751,914t @ 1.4g/t head grade, 94% recovery, produced 32,124oz** [D009, p.48–49]. FY27 grade assumption and White Devil strip ratio not in D009 — stays open.

**Q21: Confirm Fairview 47,678oz vs table 47,677oz rounding. [D003, p.6–7]**
- **Status**: `Unresolved — not addressed in D009`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 aggregates Barberton Mines total (71,997oz produced / 70,086oz sold) without a Fairview sub-line [D009, p.49]. Carry forward.

**Q37: Confirm Tennant FY27 capex guidance; crusher/mill/filter complete or continuing. [D006.2, p.22; D006.6, p.29]**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved (baseline only)`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 confirms FY26 Tennant capex = **US$71.3m** [D009, p.50]. FY27 guidance and crusher/mill/filter completion status not in D009 — stays open.

**Q45: Confirm FY26 Tennant full-year production/grade to anchor FY27 run-rate. [D006.2, p.23]**
- **Status**: `Resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 confirms Tennant FY26 **produced 32,124oz / sold 32,124oz**, surface feedstock **751,914t @ 1.4g/t**, **94% recovery** [D009, p.48–49]. FY25 comparator: 1,513oz produced (first year) [D009, p.49].

**Q46: Confirm July 2026 deliveries against contract liability 8,030 included in FY27 ounces/revenue, not double-counted. [D006.3, p.25; D006.10, p.35]**
- **Status**: `Unresolved — not addressed in D009`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 shows FY26 Tennant sold = produced = 32,124oz (no deferred delivery visible) [D009, p.49]. Contract liability treatment not in D009 — carry forward.

**Q48: Reconcile Australia revenue 132,804 to Tennant segment revenue in D006.2. [D006.3, p.25, fn 1]**
- **Status**: `Partially resolved`
- **Answer & evidence**: D009 shows Tennant FY26 revenue = **A$195.3m** [D009, p.50]. At the stated average US$/A$ rate of **1.47** [D009, p.50], A$195.3m ÷ 1.47 = **~US$132.9m**, consistent with the US$132,804k Australia revenue figure. Confirms the FX translation. Remainder (tie to D006.2 segment revenue) stays open.

### 2. Footnote & Qualification Deep-Dive

**No footnote markers (`[^1]`, `*`, note references) are present in the supplied D009 evidence** (pp. 48–50). The tables are presented without footnote definitions or small-print qualifications in this capture.

However, several **embedded table qualifications and structural anomalies** function as de facto caveats and must be flagged:

1. **"Head grade – excluding third-party buy-in feedstock" row** [D009, p.48]: This is a critical qualification. Evander's headline total head grade is **4.8g/t**, but excluding third-party buy-in feedstock it is **11.2g/t**. The 202,555t of third-party buy-in at Evander (vs 34,411t FY25) massively dilutes the reported grade and drives the 4.6g/t overall recovered grade. **Implication**: Evander's underground grade quality (>11g/t) is masked by toll-milling/buy-in activity. For forecasting, the underground grade is the sustainable metric; the buy-in feedstock is a margin/volume variable. Must verify in D003/D006.2 whether buy-in feedstock is expected to continue at FY26 levels in FY27.

2. **"US$ all-in sustaining cost – excluding third-party buy-in feedstock" row** [D009, p.49]: Group AISC excluding buy-in = **US$1,798/oz** vs headline **US$1,867/oz**. This confirms buy-in feedstock **raises** group AISC by ~US$69/oz (i.e., buy-in is margin-dilutive at current prices). Evander AISC excluding buy-in = **US$1,698/oz** vs headline **US$1,914/oz** — a US$216/oz difference. **Implication**: If buy-in feedstock volumes fall in FY27, group AISC should improve materially. Must verify FY27 buy-in assumptions in D003/D006.2.

3. **Tennant FY25 AIC of US$32,041/oz** [D009, p.49]: This is a nonsensical-looking figure driven by the 1,513oz ramp-up year against fixed costs. It confirms FY26 (US$4,339/oz) is the first meaningful full-year AIC. **Implication**: FY25 comparatives for Tennant are not forecast-relevant; use FY26 as the base.

4. **Tennant "surface feedstock" vs "tailings" classification** [D009, p.48–49]: Tennant's 751,914t is classified as **surface feedstock** (not tailings), processed at 94% recovery — a different processing route from the SA tailings operations (33–47% recovery). **Implication**: Tennant's cost/grade/recovery profile is structurally different; do not blend with SA tailings metrics.

5. **Capex total discrepancy**: D009 group capex = **US$214.9m** [D009, p.50] vs stored FY26 total of **224.0** [D008.3, p.44]. A ~US$9.1m gap exists — likely corporate/exploration capex not allocated to operations in D009. Must reconcile in D006.5/D008.3.

6. **Revenue table anomaly**: D009 shows Barberton Mines total revenue ZAR 5,958.6m but the "Mining operations Barberton" column shows 5,052.1 and Evander 3,397.5 — the "Barberton Mines total" (5,958.6) appears to include BTRP (906.5) but the arithmetic 5,052.1 + 906.5 = 5,958.6 confirms this. Similarly Evander Mines total 7,452.6 = 3,397.5 + 4,055.1 (Elikhulu). **Implication**: The "Mines total" columns are additive roll-ups including surface operations; do not double-count.

7. **Gold produced total rounding**: Underground produced 105,479oz but group total shows 105,478oz [D009, p.48] — 1oz rounding difference. Immaterial but noted.
